- **Prettier cannot be adopted here as things stand.** The tree is hand-formatted (dense one-line JSX, long lines) and `cargo fmt --check` reports thousands of diffs; a formatter gate means one enormous commit and a fight with the house style afterwards, and it rewrites a whole file the moment a lane touches one line. `scripts/check-format.mjs` takes the mechanical half instead (BOM, NUL, trailing whitespace, mixed line endings, invalid UTF-8) and leaves Markdown alone: two trailing spaces are a hard break, and `docs/library/` is generated. The one exception in its allowlist is the deliberate NUL in `crates/retinue-storage/src/lib.rs` (`!seed.contains('\u0000')` written as a raw byte).
- **A tracked git hook does not survive `core.autocrlf=true`**: the file arrives with CRLF and `sh` refuses it ("bad interpreter"). Write the hook into `.git/hooks` from an installer (`scripts/install-hooks.mjs`) with an explicit `\n`, and keep the logic in a tracked `scripts/*.mjs` that the hook calls. `node --test scripts/` fails with `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`; the glob form `node --test "scripts/**/*.test.mjs"` is what works (and it collects the three `.test.mjs` files that existed before and were never run by any gate).

Timeout note: the daemon's own build scripts are still blocked by Smart App Control on this machine (os error 4551), so a fresh target is compiled by deleting the blocked `target\debug\build\<crate>-*` folder and retrying, one crate at a time.

## 2026-11-16 · Daemon · "OpenCode sign-in expired" was really an empty pay-as-you-go balance

- **Symptom:** every turn in one Ace (CEO, OpenCode) conversation failed in 2–3 s with "The coding agent's sign-in expired… run opencode auth login". `opencode auth list` showed both credentials stored, `opencode run` worked, and new Grey and Ace conversations answered.
- **Real error** (only in OpenCode's own log, `%USERPROFILE%\.local\share\opencode\log\opencode.log`, "Failed to drain Session"): "Insufficient balance… billed to OpenCode Models credits… OpenCode Go covers only requests to the OpenCode Go provider."
- **Cause 1, the model lock:** that conversation's `sessions.model_lock_json` (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Retinue\retinue.db`) held `opencode/deepseek-v4.1-flash` (Zen, pay-as-you-go), not `opencode-go/…`. Changing the bot's model doesn't change an existing conversation's lock.
- **Cause 2, the wrong label:** the error carries a billing URL with a 26-character workspace id. `retinue_security::contains_secret_marker` treats any 40+ character key-like word as a secret, so `safe_provider_error_detail` (`retinue-daemon/src/main.rs`) swapped the whole text for "authentication or credential problem". `sign_in_hint` then matched "credential problem" and set `failureClass: unauthenticated`. The daemon log was empty, so nothing kept the real text.
- **Fixed (same day):** `safe_provider_error_detail` now hides only the secret-looking words (`redact_secret_words`, "[hidden]") and falls back to the generic sentence only if a marker survives. `failure_detail_and_sign_in` judges the sign-in from the raw text, `sign_in_hint` no longer matches "credential problem", and "401" must be a whole word.
- **Owner rule (16 November 2026): never OpenCode pay-as-you-go.** Only Zen's free models (`opencode/*-free`, `opencode/big-pickle`) and OpenCode Go when linked (`gatewayAuthStates["opencode-go"] == "authenticated"`; an empty map leaves it to OpenCode). `catalog::keep_opencode_included_models` filters every served catalog (`get`, `cached`, prewarm compare), so cached lists follow it too. `validate_exec_catalog` refuses a paid Zen model even in an old conversation (`OPENCODE_PAID_ZEN_MESSAGE`). Other providers configured in OpenCode with the owner's own keys are untouched.
- **How to diagnose next time:** read `opencode.log` near the turn's `startedAt`, and check the conversation's `model_lock_json`.

## 2026-11-16 · Pipeline · Iris (Codex) can generate images but often can't save them into the repo

- **Symptom:** asked through the Retinue MCP to save WebP wallpapers into `apps/desktop/public/background`, one Iris conversation saved them and two said the only shell was a cloud container without the project folder.
- **Where they are:** Codex keeps every generated image as a PNG in `%USERPROFILE%\.codex\generated_images\<thread id>\exec-<id>.png`, named as in the conversation's "Generated an image" notice. Ask the bot for a table mapping each `exec-….png` to its target name, then convert yourself. No converter is installed; `python -m pip install --target <scratchpad>\pylib pillow` works.
- **Check the files, not the report:** Iris said she had replaced `anime-1..5` with character art, but the files were still the first landscapes, and her two hand fixes were rejected by the owner. Build a contact sheet with Pillow and look before you accept a set.
- **Art direction:** say "character as the bold subject, large in frame" when the owner wants character art; "a figure in a landscape" gives tiny figures. Steer with a written description of reference images and keep characters original.

## 2026-11-15 · UI · WebGL canvases turned into sad-face squares after live reloads

- **Symptom:** in the dev app the launch logo and the top-left logo became white squares with a sad face.
- **Cause:** `reactbits/MetallicPaint.tsx` deleted its shaders and buffers on unmount but never released the WebGL context. Every hover (the top-left paint mounted on hover) and every live reload leaked one; past Chromium's limit of about 16 it kills the oldest, which draws the sad face. Making the paint always on reached the limit sooner.
- **Second, bigger cause:** `canPaintMetal()` (`ui/MetallicMark.tsx`) probed with `document.createElement('canvas').getContext('webgl2')` on every call and kept the context, and `LaunchIntro` calls it on every render; after enough of them Chromium refused WebGL in that window, so the background vanished too. A reload did not help; restarting the app did.
- **Fix:** the probe runs once per window and releases its context; dispose calls `gl.getExtension('WEBGL_lose_context')?.loseContext()`, and a lost canvas hides itself so the PNG beneath shows. The ported Lanyard calls `renderer.forceContextLoss()`. Any WebGL probe must release what it creates.
- **To avoid:** every WebGL component releases its context on unmount, and shows a still fallback when the context is lost.

## 2026-11-15 · UI · Lazy Lucide icons need their own import path

- `import('lucide-react').then((m) => m.icons)` would pull all 1,500 icons into the main bundle, because the package is imported statically everywhere. `ui/replyIcons.tsx` imports `lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/index.js` (typed in `src/lucide-icons.d.ts`), which Vite splits into its own chunk. In Vitest that path transforms 1,544 modules (about 17 s), so mock it in tests (`SafeMarkdown.mounted.test.tsx`). A Vitest module mock throws on a missing export, so the lookup catches and treats it as unknown.

## 2026-11-15 · Coding agents · A switched-off provider kept its probe failure on its row

- **Symptom:** the owner turned Cursor CLI and Grok CLI off in Settings › Coding agents, yet the app still showed "Cursor CLI · Protocol check: The CLI does not advertise the required protocol."
- **Cause:** `discover_enabled` (`crates/retinue-daemon/src/main.rs:662`) probes an off provider once when it has no saved row, so Settings can tell "installed, switched off" from "not installed". `persist_runtime_discovery` saved that row, and `stamp_runtime_state` (`main.rs:616`) set `status: "disabled"` but left `probeError`/`probeStep` on it. Every reader of the row — `runtime.list`, the Settings sheet, `doctor.rs`'s protocol row — then reported a failure for an agent that is not offered.
- **Fix:** an off stamp removes `probeError` and `probeStep` (installed, version and executablePath stay); a row an older build saved is cleaned the next time discovery stamps it. The window also ignores a probe failure on an off row (`runtimeIsOff` in `apps/desktop/src/ui/runtimeDiscovery.ts`) and skips its startup sign-in and model checks. The Doctor's `runtime_checks` reports one `ok` row, "X is turned off in Settings. Turn it back on to use it.", and checks nothing else for it (`runtime_is_off` reads `enabled: false`, `disabled: true` or `status: "disabled"`).
- **To avoid:** when a state stamp marks something off, strip every field that means "it failed" from the row, not only the status; and when you add a consumer of a runtime row, decide whether it should speak about an off one at all.

## 2026-11-14 · Tests · Backdating an `Instant` fails on a freshly booted CI runner

- **Symptom:** in CI only, `browser_contexts_close_when_the_turn_ends_and_after_ten_idle_minutes` failed ("an idle context is closed": 1, not 0) and `computer_view_refuses_a_wrong_expired_or_unlinked_ticket_without_forwarding` panicked with "overflow when subtracting duration from instant"; the same commit's tests had passed on an earlier run.
- **Cause:** a Windows `Instant` counts from boot. On a runner up for less than ten minutes, `Instant::now() - 600 s` panics, and `checked_sub` returns `None` (the browser helper then silently left the time unchanged).
- **Fix:** test helpers backdate with `checked_sub` and fall back to a test-only flag (`Opened::stale`) or to what expiry does (drop the tickets). Never write `Instant::now() - long_duration` in a test; `attention.rs:217` and `remote/push.rs:708` still do with short spans.
- **Also from this release:** clippy `let_underscore_future` caught `let _ = tokio::process::Command::new("taskkill")...status();`, which never ran: a dropped Tokio future does nothing. One-shot kills use `std::process::Command`.

## 2026-11-14 - Tools - PowerShell `Get-Content -Raw` + `Set-Content` corrupts a UTF-8 Rust file

A scripted replace on `crates/retinue-daemon/src/browser.rs` (`Get-Content -Raw`, `.Replace(...)`, `Set-Content -Encoding utf8`) turned every em dash into `â€"` and put a BOM at the top: Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads a BOM-less file as ANSI, so the bytes arrive wrong and are then written back as UTF-8. `cargo check` still passes (the damage was in comments), so it is easy to miss in review. Use the `edit` tool (exact text, byte-preserving) for any edit to a source file; when a script is unavoidable, read and write with `[System.IO.File]::ReadAllText($p, [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8)` and `New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)`. Check with `($bytes[0..3] -join ',')` (no 239,187,191) and by grepping for `â€`.

## 2026-11-14 — Daemon/UI — A live task list per turn (`session.plan`)

The owner wanted Claude Code's to-do list under every conversation. How it is built, so the next change fits it:

- **One normalized event, three provider shapes.** `AgentEvent::PlanUpdated { items: Vec<PlanItem> }` in `crates/retinue-agent-core/src/lib.rs`; `PlanItem { text, status }` with `status` `pending`/`in_progress`/`completed`. The whole list on every event (replace, never patch); an empty list clears. Helpers there: `is_plan_tool` (matches `TodoWrite`, `todowrite`, `updateTodos` … after stripping non-alphanumerics), `plan_items_from_todos` (a `{content|text, status}` array, also the ACP `entries` shape), `plan_status` (unknown → pending, never completed), caps `PLAN_ITEMS_LIMIT` 50 and `PLAN_TEXT_LIMIT` 300.
- **Where each adapter finds it.** Claude (`retinue-adapter-claude`): the `TodoWrite` `tool_use` inside the `assistant` message carries `input.todos`. Codex (`retinue-adapter-codex`): the `turn/plan/updated` notification (`params.plan[].step` / `.status` with `inProgress`), now listed in `METHODS` (`protocol.rs`). OpenCode (ACP, `retinue-adapter-acp`): the `sessionUpdate: "plan"` update (`entries[].content`/`.status`) **and** the `todowrite` `tool_call` (`rawInput.todos`) — the ACP `tool_call` has no `name`, only `title`, so the tool is recognized by `title`, and its row is emitted with `kind: "plan"`.
- **Storage and wire.** The daemon's forwarded-event match (`main.rs`, `AgentEvent::PlanUpdated`) writes `turns.data.plan` with `merge_turn_data` (replace the whole object, do not patch) and returns the tuple `("session.plan", {sessionId,turnId,items,updatedAt})`. `session_detail_parts` (`retinue-storage/src/lib.rs`) adds `'plan', json(json_extract(t.data,'$.plan'))` to the turn `json_object`, so `session.get` returns it on the turn rows. Watch the parentheses: the `json_object(` call already ends with a `)`, and `json(json_extract(...))` needs its own pair.
- **Remote.** `tiers.json` needs `"session.plan": "drop"` or the parity test fails; it is dropped like `tool.changed` and `session.context.updated`, while `session.get` still carries the stored list to a viewer.
- **Window.** `apps/desktop/src/types.ts`: `PlanItemStatus`, `PlanItem`, `TurnPlan`, `SessionTurn` (`turns?: SessionTurn[]`), the `applyEvent` branch (creates the turn row when it has not arrived) and `normalizePlanItems`. Tool rows with `kind: "plan"` are the to-do tool's own row, so the UI can hide them in favour of the list.
- **Instruction.** One line in `team.rs`'s `runtime_instructions_in` (`TASK_LIST_INSTRUCTIONS`), beside `CHART_TABLE_INSTRUCTIONS`/`OPENUI_INSTRUCTIONS`.

## 2026-11-14 — Gates — `cargo test --lib` skips the `src/bin` helpers (and the docs build rewrites line endings)

- **`cargo test -p retinue-adapter-claude --lib` fails `spawn_failure_removes_secure_instruction_pair`** with `Os { code: 2 }`: it copies `fake-icacls.exe` from beside the test binary, and `--lib` does not build `src/bin` helpers. Run the crate without `--lib` (30 tests pass).
- **`node scripts/docs-library.mjs build` writes the whole `docs/library` snapshot as LF.** With `core.autocrlf=true` git then reports ~580 modified files; the check itself only needs the rebuilt content. Convert the tree back to CRLF after building (`Text -replace "\r\n","\n" -replace "\n","\r\n"` over text files, **never** over `docs/library/assets/*.png`, which are binary and get corrupted), then `git status -- docs` shows only the real edits (`ipc-contract.md`, its snapshot, `catalog.json`, `DOCUMENTATION.html`).
- **`docs:check` is red on `main` for `apps/site/content/docs/*`** (missing `/docs/*.webp` images and `/docs/<slug>/` links). The site lane owns that; do not chase it.
- **`npm run typecheck`, `npm test` and `docs:check` need `npm ci` in the worktree.** Without it they die on `Cannot find package 'vite'` / `'marked'`.
- **Clippy is red on this baseline in code nobody touched:** `retinue-runtime/src/providers.rs:89` (`redundant_static_lifetimes`, which blocks clippy for every crate that depends on it), four `assert_eq!(…, false)` in the storage snooze tests, `review_scopes.rs:531/797` and `computer_tests.rs:368`. Use `cargo clippy -p <crate> --no-deps` to lint your own crate while that stands.
- **The full mounted vitest suite flakes under load** (8, then 4, then 1 failing file across three identical runs). Run `npx vitest run --testTimeout=30000` from `apps/desktop`; the one standing failure is `remoteBuild.test.ts > is small enough to precache on a phone` (1,749,900 bytes against a 1,500,000 budget on this branch).

## 2026-11-14 · Site · The new website in apps/site

- **Where:** `apps/site` (Next.js static export, Tailwind, React Bits, GSAP, Lenis). It replaces `apps/website`, which stays until the owner retires it. Its README lists the scripts: art optimisation, roster and badges from the app's presets, screenshots from the desktop preview with `?demo&clean` (real employees with photos), whole-page review captures.
- **WebGL:** a page holds only a few WebGL contexts, so every React Bits shader sits in `WhenVisible`; React Strict Mode is off because those shaders release their context on unmount and the development double-run then reuses a lost one.
- **Art by Iris (Codex `gpt-6-luna`, high) in the running app:** her turns end after two or three images, so feed her two or three files per message and check each with `node scripts/check-art.mjs`. Two of her conversations can run at once on different files.
- **Never write Markdown or regex through a double-quoted `sed` or `node -e` in Bash:** backticks run as commands and backslashes are eaten. Use the Write or Edit tool, or a script file.

- **Site art from Iris often has a coloured halo** (green or yellow fringe left by background removal). Shrink the alpha a few pixels and soften it with sharp (blur the alpha channel about 2 px, threshold near 235, blur 0.8) before `optimize-art.mjs`, and check the result composited on the dark background.
- **Site props must be anchored.** The owner rejected props dropped on top of screenshots and plants hanging from nothing. A prop sits beside an element on its baseline, is pressed onto an edge, or hangs from a section's top border; big edge pieces (`art/edges/`) come in from the page sides at 2xl only.
- **Grid cells holding long code overflow phones.** A `truncate` line inside a grid item still sets the item's minimum width; give the grid child `min-w-0`. Check `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` at 375 px.
- **Headless Edge captures show no WebGL** (the lanyard and shaders are blank in `review.mjs` shots). Check those in the browser pane.

## 2026-11-14: conversations moved in the sidebar when only opened

- **Symptom:** clicking or cycling through conversations moved them up or down the sidebar with no message sent.
- **Cause:** every list ordering used `session.updatedAt` (`teamSelectors.ts` `mainSessionFor`, `sideSessionsFor`, `updatedAtOf`; `sidebarOrder.ts` `latest` and `hasSettled`). The daemon writes `updated_at` on state changes, renames, model locks, error-to-completed recovery and app restarts (`state='closed'`), so opening a conversation could reorder it. `list_conversations` showed several closed conversations with the same `updatedAt` minute after a restart.
- **Fix:** one helper, `activityAt(session)` = `lastMessageAt ?? createdAt ?? updatedAt`, used by every ordering. `lastMessageAt` comes from the newest user or assistant message.
- **Avoid:** never sort a list the owner reads by `updatedAt`.

## 2026-11-14: a dialog inside the message list can't blur the window

- The old image viewer was a `position: fixed` element inside the conversation, so the side panels stacked above its backdrop. A full-window overlay must be a portal on `document.body` (`ImageLightbox`). Tests then query `document`, not the mounted host.

## 2026-11-14: React Bits

- Components come from `DavidHDev/react-bits` `src/ts-default/<Category>/<Name>/` (`gh api repos/DavidHDev/react-bits/contents/... -H "Accept: application/vnd.github.raw"`). Licence: MIT + Commons Clause (use inside the app is allowed; never ship them as a component pack). Several need `motion` and Hugeicons, which Retinue doesn't install: rewrite those in CSS (`EffortTrack`, `TurnPlan`). Copied ones live in `apps/desktop/src/ui/reactbits/` with a header and a line in `docs/legal/third-party-notices.md`.
- `MetallicPaint`'s `scale` is the pattern scale; a large value (2.2) made the logo invisible. Use the defaults. `GradientASCII` needs light colours to show on a dark page.

## 2026-11-14: too many OpenCode lanes at once

- **Symptom:** three Ada turns ended at the same minute with "The provider reported a turn error" / `OpenCode prompt failed: Internal error: OpenCode server is unavailable`, and no report; another ended "completed" with no closing message.
- **Cause:** seven OpenCode conversations at once, each running `cargo build`/`cargo test` of the daemon in its own worktree (its own target), on one PC.
- **Fix:** keep about three to five OpenCode lanes at once. Resume a failed one in a **new** conversation told to work only in the old worktree's path (`git status`/`git diff` there first): continuing the long conversation failed with the 45-second start timeout. Check `turn_metrics` (`outcome`, `failureClass`) before trusting "done".

## 2026-11-14 · Skills · Every conversation's first prompt carries the skills index now

Symptom: 25 daemon tests failed with a `<retinue_skills>…</retinue_skills>` block glued onto the text they expected (`"Do it<retinue_skills>…"`), fan-out replies came back as `"handled: <prompt><retinue_skills>…"`, and the hiring tests listed an extra `retinue` skill. Cause: the built-in `retinue` skill (`skills.rs`, `BUILTINS`, served from memory) is listed for **every** bot, so `index_for_prompt` now returns a block even when the pool holds no imported skill; it is a per-turn prompt suffix placed after the memory catalog and before the reference and reply blocks (`main.rs`, the `prompt_text` format string). It is sent once per session per change, so it lands on each conversation's first prompt. Fix/avoid: assert message text through `sent_text()` in `mod phase2a_tests` (main.rs), which strips the block; keep the raw prompt where delivery itself matters (`the_team_queue_survives_a_restart_and_is_delivered_once_free` asserts the first prompt contains it); filter `row["builtin"] != true` when a test counts the skills an employee chose (`skills_on` in `hire_options_tests.rs`); and never index `skills.list()[0]` for an imported skill, because the built-ins are listed first. `skills.list` rows carry `source: "builtin"` and `builtin: true`.

## 2026-11-14 - Events - When an existing read gains a side channel, its first read must not emit

`computer.status` also carries the browser's readiness now (`browser::add_status`, called from the dispatch arm in `main.rs`). The first version emitted `browser.changed` whenever the value differed from "nothing announced yet", so the very first `computer.status` after boot queued an extra event and broke `phase2a_tests::computer_status_is_announced_only_when_it_changes` (`crates/retinue-daemon/src/computer_tests.rs:289`, "no event for an unchanged read") — a test owned by another lane and nothing to do with the browser. Fix: the first read only records the value (the caller already has it in the reply it asked for) and later reads emit only on a real change (`browser::announce`). Rule: when an existing read method gains an event, leave that method's event behaviour as it was; a status reply is not an announcement. Run the whole crate's tests, not only your module's, or another lane's test is where you find out.

## 2026-11-14 - Media - A tool that stores a picture must answer with the media row's id, not its own call id

`media::requested` (`crates/retinue-daemon/src/media.rs:469`) chooses the row id itself (`Storage::new_media_id`) unless a row already exists for the tool call id, and it returns nothing. `crates/retinue-daemon/src/browser.rs` passed a fresh `browser-<uuid>` as the tool call id and put that same string in its reply as `mediaId`, so the row existed under one id while the transcript and the window looked for another: the image card had nothing to load. Symptom: `browser_screenshot_is_stored_as_media_and_never_in_the_reply` failed at `media_by_id(...).expect("one media row")`. Fix: after `requested`, read the row back with `media_by_call(session_id, &call_id)` and reply with `row["id"]`; a row that is not `ready` is an error, not a broken card. Rule: the call id is how you find the row, the row id is what you tell everyone else.

## 2026-11-14 - Tests - An included `*_tests.rs` shares the module it is included into

`crates/retinue-daemon/src/main.rs` ends `phase2a_tests` with `include!("computer_tests.rs")` and so on, which is textual: every `use` and helper in those files lands in one module. A new test file that writes `use std::time::Duration as TestDuration;` fails with `E0252: the name TestDuration is defined multiple times` because `secrets_tests.rs` (included earlier) already aliases it, and a `use base64::Engine as _;` is reported as *unused* for the same reason. Symptom first seen while adding `browser_tests.rs` (14 November 2026). Fix: reuse what the earlier includes bring in, and fully qualify the rest (`std::time::Duration::from_secs(601)`); helpers defined in an earlier include (`state`, `fake_harbor`, `harbor_body`, `link_directly`, `HARBOR_TOKEN`, `TestMutex`) are already in scope, so a new file needs no `use` of its own for them. Put a new include after the one whose helpers it borrows.

## 2026-11-13 · Lanes · Eight backend lanes at once: what merging them took

Eight backend lanes ran in parallel in the in-app backend bot, one git worktree each (12 November 2026). What mattered:

- **Retinue runs four bots at once by default** (`concurrency.botsAtOnce`, 1 to 16, the owner's setting). A message past the limit is refused with "this one will start when one finishes" and is **not** queued: the conversation is created empty. Ask the owner to raise the limit, or send again when a slot frees.
- **Two of eight lanes ended with "tests are running, I'll report" and no report.** Their work was complete in the worktree. Read the diff; do not wait for the message.
- **Lanes edit shared files.** Six touched `main.rs` and all touched `docs/ipc-contract.md`. Merge by exporting each lane's diff (`git add -N` its new files first) and applying them in turn with `git apply --3way` in one integration worktree that already has a warm `target`. Almost every conflict was two lanes adding beside each other (keep both); the real one was two lanes each asserting the other's provider could not steer.
- **A lane's tests can contradict another lane's feature.** After merging, one test still used Claude as "an agent that cannot steer". Run the merged suite, not each lane's.
- **Lanes drift outside the brief** (skill files, generated docs, bridge files). Export only the paths the brief allowed.

## 2026-11-13 · Gates · Smart App Control blocks some freshly built binaries, not all

It is intermittent, not total: on 12 and 13 November `cargo check -p retinue-daemon` and the daemon and storage unit tests built and ran (1,033 and 164 passed), while several integration test binaries (`acp_lifecycle`, `acp_steering`, `table_providers`, `wire_replay`, `daemon_smoke`, `parent_watchdog`) and every build script of the native shell (`tray-icon`, `tauri-runtime-wry`, `retinue-desktop`) were refused with os error 4551. So on this machine the native shell cannot be compiled at all, and native changes are only proven by CI or a release build. Use `cargo test --no-fail-fast`, or one blocked binary hides every suite after it. The opt-in live Claude test did run: `RETINUE_LIVE_TESTS=1 cargo test -p retinue-adapter-claude --test live_suite claude_live_permission_change_and_steer` passed, which is the proof that steering reaches a real Claude Code turn.

**Workaround when cargo stops on a blocked build script (13 November 2026):** delete that build script's folder and retry, so cargo compiles it afresh — `Remove-Item -Recurse -Force 'target\debug\build\getrandom-<hash>','target\debug\build\serde_json-<hash>'`, then the same `cargo test` passed; the newly compiled script was allowed where the old binary was not. A fresh binary compiled outside the repo (`cargo new` in `C:\tmp`) ran at once, so the block is per binary, not per path. Retry before giving up on a suite.

## 2026-11-13 · Gates · Two daemon tests fail when run from inside the app's own environment

`doctor::tests::database_and_hook_rows_are_one_line_and_actionable` and `remote::tests::an_absent_web_folder_serves_the_built_in_page` failed (1045 of 1048 passed) when `cargo test -p retinue-daemon --bin retinued` ran in a lane started by the installed app. Cause: the lane inherits the running app's environment — `RETINUE_HOOK_BRIDGE` and `RETINUE_REMOTE_WEB_DIR` — so `hook_checks` finds a hook where a test binary has none, and `StaticFiles::from_env` loads the real `remote-web` folder instead of the built-in page. With both variables removed, both tests pass. Check `Get-ChildItem Env: | Where-Object Name -like '*RETINUE*'` before believing a daemon test failure. `machines_tests::one_offline_machine_does_not_hold_up_another` failed once in the full run and passed alone (known load flake).

## 2026-11-11 · Gates · Run the Rust tests with a space-free TEMP; `cargo fmt --check` is not a gate

On this machine `TEMP` is the 8.3 path `C:UsersSICOVI~1...`, so five daemon tests that compare paths fail (`repository`, `worktree_cleanup`, `machines_tests::take_over`, `session_repository`), and the long path has a space, which makes clone tests refuse. Run `cargo test -p retinue-daemon -p retinue-storage` with `TEMP` and `TMP` set to a real space-free folder (`C:
tmp`): 992 and 163 pass (11 November 2026). `cargo fmt --all -- --check` reports thousands of diffs in untouched files (no `rustfmt.toml`; the code is hand-formatted), so it is not a gate and must not be "fixed" by reformatting. Rust does build here: the Smart App Control block of 10 November did not recur.

## 2026-11-11 · Work log · A turn that ended kept saying "Working"

Symptom: a finished turn's work-log line read "Working · Ran 153 commands" for ever. Cause: `groupConversationActivity` (`ui/conversation.ts`) took any step whose last report was "running" at its word, and a turn that ends while its sub-agents are still open leaves such steps behind. Fix: the function takes what is live (false, true, or the running turn's id); a step in a turn that is not running is shown as "Did not finish". While a turn runs, only the newest group or the one with its turn id can be working.

## 2026-11-11 · Daemon · A start timeout of 45 seconds fails a long resumed conversation

A message sent into a very long OpenCode conversation failed with `failureClass: timeout` and no tool calls: `forward_events` (`main.rs`) stops a turn that has not started answering in 45 seconds. Until that is tuned, start long tasks in a new conversation, and start two OpenCode conversations about a minute apart.

## 2026-11-11 · Composer · The queue could vanish on first load: the first snapshot overwrote it

Symptom: in the preview, `?scene=queue` sometimes showed no queued messages until one was added. Cause: `loadQueue` (`ui/App.tsx`) had already stored the queue when the first-load path ran `setSnapshot(initial)` again (React StrictMode remounts in dev; any second initial load does it), and a fresh snapshot carries no queues. Nothing reloaded it, because the conversation and connection had not changed. Fix: that write goes through `carryQueues(initial, current)`, like every other snapshot refresh. Rule: never write a whole snapshot without `carryQueues`.

Also learned: "Send now" from a queue row is two existing calls in the window, `session.steer` then `session.queue.delete`, in that order so a failure leaves the message in the queue. A plain refusal (`unsupported`, `conflict`) falls back to `session.queue.send_now`. This is a stopgap until the daemon does it in one step. `session.limit.resume` and `session.limit.snooze` exist only in the preview bridge. Editing a queued message in the box writes through the draft store, so a `beforeunload` handler restores the earlier draft before the store flushes on `pagehide`. A daemon error code is the prefix of the message (`conflict: ...`), which is what a test must throw.

## 2026-11-09 · Employees · Every preset employee wears its own profile photo

Profile photos are `apps/desktop/src/blob/photos/photo-NN.webp`, 256px crops of the owner's renders in `assets/profile` (the oldest file is `photo-01`; crop a 78% square at 11% from the left and 3.7% from the top, WebP quality 82). The renders are numbered by file modification time, so new ones get the next numbers; on 10 November 2026, photos 55–80 were added this way. `PROFILE_PHOTOS` picks up every file in the folder with no code change, and `valid_photo` accepts any two- or three-digit `photo-NN`. `assets/profile` (the source renders, about 140 MB) is not in git, so only this machine can recrop them; a clone has the crops and needs nothing else. Each preset in `apps/desktop/src/team/presets.json` names its `photo`. The daemon puts it in the hired employee's look (`presets.rs` `look()`) and on CEO hire proposals, and `valid_look` accepts `photo` as `photo-NN` or a base64 PNG, JPEG or WebP data URL of at most 160,000 characters (`valid_photo`). The window shows presets with `BlobCanvas` and `presetLook()`, not `HazeMark`. **To avoid:** adding a look field in the window only; the daemon drops any look key `valid_look` does not list.

## 2026-11-09 · Rebrand · Retinue starts from a clean history; the old names are gone

The product is Retinue. On 9 November 2026 the owner archived the old history as the branch `archive/pre-retinue`, renamed the GitHub repository to `sico-vibes/retinue` and restarted `main` from one clean commit. Nothing in `main` names the earlier products any more.

- The data migration was removed, with the owner's consent: the desktop module that moved old folders, identifiers, the database file and autostart, the browser-cache key migration, and the old look ids in storage and backup import. A machine still on an old beta moves its data once with a script kept outside the repository, or starts fresh.
- Cloud dictation's `originator` header is now `retinue`. That value is unverified against the live endpoint; if transcription starts failing with a challenge, check this first.
- **To avoid:** reintroducing an old name in code, docs or tests. The release-name history lives only in `archive/pre-retinue`.

## 2026-11-08 · Storage · A new proposal kind needs two lists and a CHECK

Adding `hire` (Phase 9) failed at run time with "The proposal could not be recorded: invalid agent", although the `proposals` table had been rebuilt with the new kind. Three places decide a kind: the table's `CHECK(kind IN (...))` in the DDL **and** in the rebuild branch of `ensure_proposal_schema`, the `ddl.contains("'<kind>'")` test that decides whether to rebuild at all, and the plain allowlist in `Storage::insert_proposal` (`lib.rs`, one array of kind names). The third one raises `InvalidAgent`, which the daemon reports as a validation failure rather than a schema problem. **To avoid:** grep for every list of proposal kinds (`"permission", "merge"`) when adding one, and add a test that only creates that kind (the storage suite alone found nothing, because `ensure_proposal_schema` passed).

## 2026-11-08 · Looks · `valid_look` refuses a `style` key, so a preset cannot carry `{"style":"haze"}`

Haze is a *look theme*, chosen in the UI; the stored `agents.look` is `{shape, seed, traits, bot, art, paint, icon}` and `valid_look` rejects any other key. A Haze portrait is therefore `{"shape":"circle","icon":"char-..."}`. The same shape is what `normalizeLook`/`agentLook` in `apps/desktop/src/blob/look/index.ts` read back. **To avoid:** before writing a look from the daemon, check it against `valid_look`, not against the UI's `BlobLook` type.

## 2026-11-08 · Native shell · Thin lines crossed the window: a frame drawn before the window-state restore

**Symptom:** on first open, thin light lines ran down through the chat and across near the bottom, framing a rectangle smaller than the window, and then stayed.

**Root cause:** the main window is `transparent: true` and was created `visible: true` at the configured 1360×860 (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`). `tauri-plugin-window-state` restores the saved geometry on the window-created event, which happens *after* the first frame is drawn (`tauri-plugin-window-state-2.5.0/src/lib.rs` 198–238: position, then size, then maximised, then `show`). A transparent WebView2 keeps the edges of the frame it painted at the configured size, so the frame and the window disagreed.

**Fix:** the window starts `visible: false`; the plugin is built with `StateFlags::all().difference(StateFlags::VISIBLE)`, so size, position and maximised still restore but visibility never does; the front end calls the new `main_window_ready` command from a 30 ms timer after its first render (`apps/desktop/src/main.tsx`; a timer, not animation frames, because a hidden WebView2 may not run them), which shows and focuses the window once (`static MAIN_WINDOW_READY`, label `main` only); and a setup thread shows the window anyway after 2.5 s if the call never comes, so a stuck page or an older bundle cannot leave the app invisible. Tray, single-instance and `show_main_window` are untouched.

**To avoid:** never create a transparent window at one size and resize it after its first paint — restore the geometry while the window is hidden, and make the first frame the one the person sees. When adding a command only the main window may call, copy `set_approval_badge`: take a `WebviewWindow` argument and reject anything whose `window.label()` is not `"main"`. **Application commands are not in the Tauri v2 ACL** — the generated `apps/desktop/src-tauri/gen/schemas/desktop-schema.json` has no row for `daemon_rpc` or `set_approval_badge` — so a capability entry for one is neither possible nor needed, and the label check is the whole restriction.

## 2026-11-01 · Storage · A correlated subquery made every conversation cost 250 to 400 ms

`session_detail_parts` read each turn's `turn.error` detail with a correlated subquery inside the turns statement. `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` shows `SCAN e` for that form, while the same lookup with the turn id bound as `?1` is `SEARCH … USING INDEX idx_app_events_turn_error`. SQLite would not seek the partial index for the correlated term, so it scanned the whole event log **once per turn**: 11.5 to 14.3 s for the log's 167 turns, about 74 ms a turn. A conversation with one turn cost about 75 ms and a busy one 1.2 s, although every statement measured on its own stayed under 10 ms — which is why the cost looked unexplainable and was blamed on the 380 MB of tool payloads instead.

Measured on a copy of the owner's 654 MB database (67 conversations, 4,972 tool calls, 150,966 events, 167 turns), `cargo run --release -p retinue-storage --example snapshot-timing -- <copy> --runs 5`:

| measure | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `session.get`, median | 81 ms | **0.5 ms** |
| slowest conversation | 1,211 ms | **100 ms** (96 ms of it the tool payloads) |
| per-turn `turn.error` lookup, all 167 turns | 11,587 ms (`SCAN e`) | **1.7 ms** (`SEARCH … USING INDEX`) |
| full snapshot | 12.4 s | **1.0 s** |

Fix: read the turns without `detail`, then run one indexed lookup per turn with the id bound, and merge the value in (valid JSON stays a value, anything else stays a string, exactly as `json_object` embedded it). `turnDetailProof` in the tool proves `answersIdentical: true` for all 167 turns on the owner's data.

**To avoid:** never put a correlated subquery over `app_events` inside a per-row statement. Print the plan of the **whole** statement — a probe that times the inner `SELECT` alone shows the index being used and hides the scan. `--plans` in `snapshot-timing` prints the shapes side by side.

## 2026-11-01 · Architecture · Still slow after beta.48: what it loads and what it waits on

Second half of the day: measured on a copy of the owner's database (654 MB, 67 conversations, 4,972 tool calls, 167 turns) with `crates/retinue-storage/examples/snapshot-timing.rs`. The installed beta.48 felt like a heavy backend because of what the window loaded and what an action waited on, not because of Rust, Tauri or SQLite.

| measure | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| lazy snapshot (`app.snapshot?lazy`) | 8.1–8.8 s (keep 15); 4.96 s on this copy | **99 ms**, 2.1 MB (keep 0) |
| full snapshot | 21.8–22.5 s (12.4 s on this copy) | **1.0 s**, 13 MB |
| `session.get`, median | 81 ms | **0.5 ms** |
| effort save (`agent.update` + `session.model.update`, storage work) | 226 ms | **3.5 ms** |
| the same while every conversation is read back to back | 630 ms | **3.7 ms** |
| `task.create` + `task.dispatch` | 3.2 ms | 3.4 ms (the queue was never the cost; the writer lock was) |
| a write during a full background load | queued behind a snapshot (Settings waited up to 33.8 s in the first wave) | **0 ms median, 0.1 ms max** |

What changed:

- **Three read connections, not one.** `snapshot_reader`, `read_conn` (settings, `agent_get`, `session_event_summary`, `session_execution_row`) and `detail_conn` (a whole conversation). `session_detail`, `session_event_summary` and `agent_get` no longer take the writer lock. Every read runs in its own transaction, so read-after-write stays correct; `impl Drop` still closes readers first so the writer checkpoints the log.
- **A conversation loads when it is opened.** `App.tsx` fetched every partial conversation four at a time; now `conversationsToLoad` loads the open one plus the selected bot's newest two, one at a time, and nothing in the companion. A `session.changed` loads a transcript only for a conversation on screen — the row arrives in the event and `applyEvent` already merges it.
- **Startup sends summaries only.** `LAZY_SNAPSHOT_KEEP` is 0, so every at-rest conversation is a row plus its last three messages and no `tool_calls` row is read. A conversation with a turn running is still sent in full, because the window has nothing else to show.
- **An action reads the row, not the transcript.** `Storage::session_execution_row` is the conversation's own row plus its context counters; `session.model.update`, `session.model.plan` and `session.permissions.set` use it.
- **Effort is instant and honest.** The pill shows the chosen level while the save runs (`requestEffort` keeps the draft until it resolves; `triggerLabel` follows the draft), the bot and conversation writes run together (`Promise.allSettled`), and `agent.update` reads the model catalog **once**, from the cache (`Mode::CachedOnly`), instead of `Fresh` twice under two different Claude cache keys (one without the model hint, one with). A model change still writes the conversation first, so a cancelled one leaves both rows untouched. When no catalog is cached the values are accepted, which is what a failed probe already did.
- **A dropped task reads "Starting" at once**, before `task.dispatch` answers (`pendingDispatch`, UI only, never sent by the daemon), and goes back with the reason if it is refused.
- Still open: the tool payloads. 185 tool rows are 96 of one conversation's 100 ms, and 901 ms of the 993 ms the whole database costs to detail; the read-only-ids comparison is 17.8 ms. Fixing that needs `tool_calls` columns for kind/title/state and a tool-output-on-demand RPC, not a faster query.

**To avoid:** when a screen feels slow, time the RPC end to end on a copy before optimising any statement, and check which lock the action waits on. A per-conversation read on the writer lock queues every save behind it, however fast the query is.

## 2026-11-01 · Tests · An `Instant` can't go further back than the machine's uptime

`backup_import_of_an_expired_preview_is_not_found` failed in CI on `9f8fdae` and passed locally: the import succeeded after `expire_all`. The helper did `Instant::now().checked_sub(TTL + 1 s).unwrap_or_else(Instant::now)`. On Windows an `Instant` counts from boot, so on a runner up for less than 10 minutes `checked_sub` returns `None` and the preview is simply "created now". Inferred from the code; the runner's uptime wasn't read. Fix: store when a preview expires, not when it was made, so a test sets `expires` to now. Avoid: never fake the past by subtracting from an `Instant`.

## 2026-11-01 · Performance · The app feels slow everywhere at once: one lock, one huge snapshot

Symptom: skeleton loaders and "unsnappy" throughout, not tied to any one screen. Root cause, measured on a copy of the owner's real database (`<data>/retinue.db`, then 510 MB):

- `Storage` held **one** `Mutex<Connection>`, so every read queued behind the longest one.
- `app.snapshot` was the longest: lazy (15 fully detailed conversations) **304 ms and 3.1 MB of JSON**, full **1,297 ms and 9.6 MB**.
- Inside that, the per-session `tool_calls` query was a **full scan**: 383 MB in 3,973 rows (≈96 KB each of provider payload), with no index on `session_id`. With one: 318 ms → 137 ms. Reading only the ids: **3.4 ms**.

**Second wave, same day (done).** Numbers from `cargo run --release -p retinue-storage --example snapshot-timing -- <copy> --runs 3 --concurrent 20 --index` on a copy of the owner's **548 MB** database (65 open conversations, 4,310 tool calls, 141,692 events, 160 turns). The 304 ms figure above is not reproducible: this machine and this data give seconds. Measurements here vary ±30% with the page cache, so compare medians of three:

| measure | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| lazy snapshot | 8,080–8,828 ms (median) | 5,335 ms |
| full snapshot | 21,771–22,493 ms | 17,451 ms |
| `session_context_locked`, all 65 conversations | 2,172–2,982 ms (`SCAN app_events`) | 2.2 ms (`SEARCH … USING INDEX`) |
| `turn.error` detail read, all 160 turns | 23,209 ms (`SCAN e`) | 2.8 ms (`SEARCH … USING INDEX`) |
| `settings.get` while a snapshot runs | max 19,382–33,813 ms | max 8.6 ms |
| creating both indexes (once, at startup) | — | 143 ms + 115 ms |

- **A partial expression index on `app_events` is the fix for a `json_extract` lookup.** The `WHERE` must repeat every condition the query has — `event_type IN (…)` **and** `json_valid(payload)`, because `json_extract` raises on malformed JSON — and the query must say the same, or the planner silently keeps the scan. `ensure_read_indexes` now creates `idx_app_events_session_context` and `idx_app_events_turn_error`; `session_detail_parts`' turns query gained `json_valid(e.payload)` to match (proved identical for every turn on the real data).
- **Two read connections, not one shared one.** `app.snapshot` gets its own, and the small Settings reads (`settings.get`, the approval policy, `runtime.profile.list`) get another: one reader for both would have put Settings behind the snapshot again. In-memory databases have no second connection, so they fall back to the old single-connection path. Every read runs in its own transaction, which is also what makes a read see a write committed a moment earlier.
- **A read-only connection cannot checkpoint the log.** With readers open at close, the last connection to go is a reader, so `-wal` and `-shm` stayed on disk beside the database (`v1_migration_…rolls_back_in_temp_paths` failed with a stale log applied to a restored file). `impl Drop for Storage` closes the readers first, so the writer is still last and folds the log in.
- **`PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` now runs on every file open**, not only the fresh-database branch; the readers rely on it.
- **`agent.update` only reads the model catalog when the save touches `runtimeId`/`model`/`thinking`/`serviceTier`.** `catalog::get(Mode::Fresh)` can start a provider probe, and an icon save was paying for it (and could silently fill in an effort level). The lock check stays first, and `validate_agent_catalog` still runs for the fields it validates.
- **A full `refresh()` after every action is what made the window feel slow**: it re-reads the whole snapshot. The send path, pull-request and review-comment sends, project creation, project restore and a scheduled conversation delete now rely on the reply plus the live events; `replay.gap` still reloads (that is the recovery), with a 250 ms window that turns a burst into one reload. Undo of a conversation delete still reloads: the snapshot is what puts the row back in its place.
- **An icon shows before the save:** a project's icon and a bot's look/colour are applied to the snapshot at once, and put back with the daemon's reason if the save is refused.
- **Not fixed, and measured:** one streamed token costs a whole-message rewrite plus a `message.delta` row: **2.6–11.6 ms** each on this database (`synchronous=FULL`, an fsync per commit). Batching it needs a flush before every non-delta event of that session to keep replay order, and a crash would drop the buffered tail of a partial message, so it was left. What is left of a snapshot is the per-conversation detail: 250–400 ms each, while each of its queries is under 10 ms on its own — the time goes on the ~380 MB of tool payloads the snapshot returns in full. That is the next thing to chase, and it needs the snapshot to carry less, not a faster query.
- **A UI test can depend on `refresh()` without saying so.** `App.emptyConversation` saw its auto-title only through the reloaded snapshot; with the reload gone the fixture has to emit the `session.changed` the daemon really sends.

**To avoid:** copy the database to a temp dir and time the exact SQL before optimising; never open the live file. Also: SQLite refuses `rowid` inside `CREATE INDEX` ("no such column: rowid") — index the column alone. For a read path nobody has timed end to end, write the timing tool first (`crates/retinue-storage/examples/snapshot-timing.rs`), and never edit an `app_events` query without re-reading the plan it prints.

## 2026-11-01 · Native shell · Every app start copied a 39 MB binary, and left the old ones

`start_daemon` copied `retinued.exe` to a new UUID name on every start and removed it only if the app was alive when the daemon died: one install had **18 stale copies, 557 MB**, and the copy itself measured **333 ms warm / 600 ms cold**, synchronously on the main thread. Now the copy is named by the source's size and modification time and reused while the build is the same, other copies are pruned, and the stop paths no longer delete it. It is written as `retinued-<pid>.partial` and renamed: reuse trusts the size, and an interrupted copy under the final name could pass that check and fail every later start.

Same entry: `rpc_call` built a **new `reqwest::Client` per request** (no pooling); it uses one shared client. `select_project_folder`/`select_local_file` used `blocking_pick_*` in a sync command, which freezes every native operation (drag, tray, other commands) while the dialog is open; they are async now and wait on a worker thread.

## 2026-11-01 · Composer · `#` names a project now, not a side conversation

Owner decision: a bot works in many projects, so what a request needs to say is *where*, and `#` is the natural token. `#` in the composer now opens `ProjectPicker` (`teamSelectors.projectMatches`: exact name, then prefix, then substring, then tag). A new conversation's `projectId` is read back from the sent words (`projectNamedInText`), never kept beside them: a separate ref let the words and the folder disagree (a deleted `#Board` still opened in Board; a reopened draft showed `#Board` and opened elsewhere). Naming a bot with `@` appends `#` and opens the project step at once; that `#` is the app's, so a word no project fits, a space, Escape or sending takes it back out (`leaveProjectStep`), or "Ask @Codex fix it" goes out as "Ask @Codex #fix it". A `#` the owner typed is never removed, and `# ` with a space opens no picker. Tests that only pick from the list miss all of this: test carrying on typing too. Side conversations are still referenceable from a message's own menu (`MessageMenu` → `conversationRefs`), and `splitReferences` still renders stored ones.

The daemon resolves the same three ways in one place, `team::project_named`: id, exact name, unique tag, ignoring a leading `#`. An ambiguous tag or name is an error **naming the candidates**, so the Manager asks with real options instead of guessing; `message_blob`/`start_conversation`/`assign_task`/`propose_bot` all go through it. The Manager's instruction (team.rs) now says to read `#Name` from the message and to `ask_user` with the projects when none is named, and every bot is told that `#Name` is a project to pass on `message_blob` (an existing conversation stays in its folder). The approval card's `projectForDraft` resolves tags the same way, or a bot proposal shows the raw tag. The per-turn `<retinue_manager>` workspace block lists each project's tags.

The daemon no longer reads `Agent.defaultProject` (a path) to decide where a bot works: `external.rs project_path_of` dropped its fallback. The bot editor's "Working folder" box still writes it and `rosterSelectors.recentProjects` still lists it as a recent project, but it decides nothing — remove the box, that read and the column together when someone has the test fixtures to spare.

## 2026-11-01 · CSS · A component's stylesheet can load before `theme.css`

The icon picker rendered 1,300 px wide and the bell panel ran off the window. `main.tsx` imports `App` first, so a stylesheet imported by a component arrives before `theme.css`, and `.menu-surface` then wins any tie. Fix: give the rule more weight (`.menu-surface.project-icon-picker`). Avoid: never restyle a shared class at equal specificity from a component's own CSS. Tests can't see this; look at the screen.

## 2026-11-01 · Preview · Renaming a file blanked every page

Renaming `chatBackground.ts` left Vite serving a stale module, and every page came up empty with a 404 in the console. Restart the dev server after renaming a file. Also, `ChatBackground.tsx` and `chatBackground.ts` can't sit side by side: Windows and TypeScript treat them as one name.

## 2026-11-01 · Daemon · A stored-only project override costs three pieces

For a project field set through its own RPC (the icon, before it the trusted hash): add the key only to the stored allow list in `validate_overrides`, validate it in one storage function, write it in one `Storage` method that emits `project.changed`, copy the stored value forward in `project_update_with_identity`, and add `never` rows to `tiers.json`. `json_set(..., '$.icon', ?2)` stores a string; pass `json(?2)`. Run the daemon suite without `RETINUE_HOOK_BRIDGE` and `RETINUE_REMOTE_WEB_DIR`, which the running app sets: with them, `doctor::tests::database_and_hook_rows...` and `remote::tests::an_absent_web_folder...` fail for no code reason.

## 2026-10-31 · Inspector · `?clean` still showed What's New

`clean` stored a seen version of `9.9.9`, and `shouldShowWhatsNew` opens on any seen version that differs from the app's, so the dialog covered every screen. Clearing the key is what hides it: no seen version means a first run, which shows nothing. Fixed in `preview/fixtureBridge.ts`.

## 2026-10-31 · Focus · A search box drew two outlines

The global `:focus-visible` ring landed on the `<input>` while its wrapper drew its own `:focus-within` ring. Fix: a text field never takes the ring (`theme.css`), and wrappers change `border-color` only. Check any new wrapped field for this.

## 2026-10-31 · Release · A gate that printed its result never exited

The beta.46 release sat for 41 minutes on `npm run perf` after it printed "every budget holds". A `claude.exe --help` probe outlived the daemon the gate had killed, and it had inherited the gate's stdout pipe, so Node waited on the pipe. `bounded_output` puts its probes in a job, so why this one survived isn't known; it's on the polish list. The gate now calls `process.exit(0)`. **To avoid it:** when a release script goes quiet, read its log's timestamp, and look at what's still running under it. CI on GitHub uses a newer Rust than this machine (1.94), so a clippy failure there may not reproduce here.

## 2026-10-31 · Phase 8: sub-bots, what to remember

- **`hidden` isn't enough to keep a sub-bot off a screen.** The sidebar, phone and companion respect `hidden`, but automations, memory, "Ask several bots", settings, search and the archived list didn't. Any new screen that offers bots as a choice must leave out `parentAgentId`.
- **Don't show a tool's title to a phone.** `doingNow` is a running tool's title, which can hold a command or a path. The `read` field watcher doesn't catch that, so `subbot.list` drops it by name. Check what a "why" in `tiers.json` claims against the real result.
- **The activity setting hid the thing the owner asked to see.** Experimental Agent activity is off by default, so nested steps alone would have stayed invisible. `isSubagentItem` rows always show. Check a feature with default settings, not only with the flag that shows it.
- **`buildConversationItems` copies named fields at the end.** A new field on `ConversationItem` is silently lost unless it's added to that last `map`.
- **Claude's `Agent` output starts with lines for the model, not the reader** ("Async agent launched successfully…", `agentId:`). `subagentOutput` removes them for display only.
- **"haiku" prefers the parent's provider,** so an OpenCode Go bot gets `opencode-go/claude-haiku-5-5`. Use the full id, `claude-haiku-5-5`, to reach Claude Code. This is the rule, not a bug.
- **`taskkill /T` isn't atomic.** It killed `ping` first, and the parent `cmd` ran the script's next step before its own turn, so Stop could let one more setup step through (`stopping_a_turn_stops_its_setup_script` under load, which had looked flaky). Setup scripts and submodule updates now run in `retinue_process::ProcessTree`, a job from the first instruction. Anything a setup script leaves running also stops when the script ends. `project_clone.rs` and `worktree_attach.rs` still use `taskkill`, which is fine for a single git command but not for a chained script.
- **`target\debug\incremental` reached 100.8 GB** and left D with 5 GB. It's only a compile cache: `rmdir` it when D runs low, and keep `deps`.
- **Fix a CI failure on the tool's real path.** The runner's temp folder is `RUNNER~1`, git reports the long name, and text comparison called live copies abandoned. `worktree_cleanup::key` now resolves the real name, and CI sets `TEMP` to `RUNNER_TEMP`.

## 2026-10-31 · Daemon · An open conversation was left working in an emptied task copy

- **Symptom:** a real OpenCode bot in a fan-out replied without changing a file, and the transcript said "Nothing was changed, but the separate copy could not be removed." The copy was not there any more.
- **What Windows really does** (live, OpenCode 2.2.2): `git worktree remove --force` deletes every file and git's own record, then fails on the folder (error 32), because each `opencode acp` process has its first conversation's folder as its working folder. The branch survives. The folder stays, empty, and `worktree.abandoned` can't list it because git no longer knows it.
- **The next message was the real damage:** it reached the same live process, the bot reported an empty folder, and it wrote its file there. That folder is not a repository, so `inspect` failed silently in `after_task_turn` and no merge was offered, so the work was stranded with no error. A Claude bot hit the same discard (Sona's copy, in the 30 October entry on Auto approval mode). Two emptied copies were still on the owner's disk on this date: `which-model-are-you` and `hi-sona-you-are-the-backend-2`.
- **Merge and Discard on a merge card did the same**, with the same false "could not be removed", and left the conversation in the emptied folder with its branch kept.
- **Fix, four parts:**
  - `after_task_turn` no longer discards. A conversation that is open can get another message, so a copy with no changes stays, and it goes on the abandoned list once the conversation is deleted or archived. **The cost:** unchanged copies now last until then, including a read-only delegation's.
  - `worktree::remove_copy` counts an empty, unregistered folder as gone, for merge and discard. A folder with anything in it is still a leftover. `tidy_emptied_folder` clears the empty folder 45 seconds later (the ACP adapter stops an idle process after 30).
  - Merge and Discard close the conversation's agent (`close_agent`). An agent stays in the folder it started in whatever the database says, so changing `projectPath` alone left the next message in the old folder.
  - `worktree::restore`, run by `before_task_turn` at the start of `run_turn`, brings back a copy already emptied or deleted, from its branch. It checks for the `.git` file first, so a normal turn spawns no git. A folder with files in it is left alone and the owner is told. The project's setup steps are not rerun, so copied files such as `.env.local` don't come back.
- **Not a neutral working folder for the process.** A probe shows that frees the folder (only the process's working folder holds it; a session alone doesn't), but it covers OpenCode only, and the copy would still be removed under an open conversation.
- **OpenCode 2 ties a session to its folder.** `session/load` from another folder fails with -32602 "session ... does not belong to cwd". That wasn't on the ACP adapter's resume-rejected list, so the first message after a merge would have failed outright. It now starts a fresh session with the recap. Only the live run could show this; a stub adapter resumes anywhere.
- **Testing:** hold the folder with a single process (`ping`, not `cmd /c ping`, or the child outlives the kill). Holding a tracked file with `share_mode(0)` makes `git add -A` fail at random when git re-reads it; hold an ignored file when a merge has to get past `git add`.

## 2026-10-30 · Daemon · Phase 1B (compare, limit-reset sends, stash, notes, new projects, problem reports): what to remember

- **A queued send to a limited conversation is only blocked for the `Queue` origin.** `session_prompt_impl` refuses `state == "limited"` unless the origin is User or Mcp, and a plain `send_later` item goes out as `User`. So "send when the limit resets" needs no special origin; what it needs is `due_tick` not claiming it early. `queue_claim_next(.., include_waiters)` and `limit_waiters_may_send` (`queue.rs`) hold it while `limitedUntil` is ahead (and move its `due_at` when the window was extended), and while the watchdog's own continuation is still to be queued (setting on, `watchdog_due_sessions` lists the session). The continuation is kind `retry` and the waiter sorts after it by `whenLimitResets` in the claim's ORDER BY.
- **Fan-out record first, rows later.** `fanout_create` runs before the conversations, so `fanoutId` (resolved through the table in the session DTOs) is valid from the first `session.changed`; `fanout_set_rows` fills the rows in at the end. Deleting the record needs no write to the conversations.
- **Child modules of the storage crate can `impl Storage`** and reach the private `conn` (`phase1b.rs`); it keeps new code out of the 10,000-line `lib.rs`, whose one NUL byte makes grep treat it as binary (use `grep -a`) and Edit the only safe editor.
- **Stubbing `gh`:** a `.cmd` (Windows) or `sh` script on a path that `scaffold::gh_program()` returns under `cfg(test)`, serialised with `GH_TEST_LOCK`. `tokio::process::Command` quotes an argument with spaces in a batch file's `%*`. git needs `GIT_AUTHOR_*`/`GIT_COMMITTER_*` set in tests that make a commit.
- **PowerShell calls don't share environment changes:** remove `RETINUE_HOOK_BRIDGE` and `RETINUE_REMOTE_WEB_DIR` in the same command that runs `cargo test`, or the two known env tests fail.
- **Python in a Bash heredoc is fine for text without backslashes** (it handled the CRLF `tiers.json`); put anything with Windows paths in a file.

## 2026-10-31 · Phase 3: git and review

- **An action that destroys two things must check the first really happened before the second.** `worktree.removeAbandoned` deleted the branch after a failed removal, losing the only handle on those commits. The unit tests all passed, and only the live smoke showed it, because a real coding agent held the folder open. Test a failed removal by holding a file with `share_mode(0)`.
- **On Windows, `git worktree remove --force` deletes the files and then fails on the empty folder while a process has it as its working folder,** and each OpenCode process's working folder is its first conversation's. So git's failure doesn't mean the copy survived, and retries keep failing. Check whether the folder is empty, then `git worktree prune`. Only a folder with files left counts as not removed.
- **A safety rule built on a list fails open when the list can't be read.** `session_list(...).unwrap_or_default()` would have made every live conversation's copy look abandoned. Return nothing when the source can't be read, not an empty list that means "nothing is using this".
- **An archived conversation can still have a running turn,** so archived alone doesn't mean its folder is free.
- **When the base branch is unknown, the default branch looks like any other.** A clone with no `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` passed every check on `main` and would have pushed it before `gh` refused. Ask `gh repo view` for the default, and refuse `main`, `master`, `trunk` and `develop` when nothing knows.
- **Old and new line numbers overlap in a diff.** A comment placed by number alone showed up on two lines. Keep which side it belongs to.
- **A refusal test must not hold a URL the validator could rescue.** `project.clone` trimmed before checking, so a refusal case with a leading space became a real network clone. Validate the exact bytes sent, and treat a stray space as a refusal.
- **A clone URL's host can read as an option:** `ssh://-oProxyCommand=...`. Refuse a host starting with a dash, on top of git's own check.
- **`Path::canonicalize()` gives `\\?\C:\...` on Windows, and git refuses that as a clone or worktree target.** Don't canonicalise temp folders in tests.
- **Walking many repositories must not spawn git per repository.** It blew a 10-second budget under load. Read `.git/HEAD` directly, following `gitdir:` for a worktree.
- **Git pathspecs:** with the global `--literal-pathspecs`, `:(literal)path` is taken literally and matches nothing, so use one or the other. In `git rev-list --remotes`, `--exclude=` globs are relative to `refs/remotes/`.
- **A `gh` stub on Windows is a `.cmd`,** so keep its arguments free of `&`, `|` and `%`.
- **A multi-line anchor in a patch script fails on CRLF files,** and a script that fails halfway has already applied its first edits. Match each file's line ending, and don't rerun an edit that landed.
- **Running the UI tests during two cargo builds failed 66 mounted tests by timeout;** the same files pass alone. Run them again when the lanes finish before looking for a bug.
- **Lanes fill D quickly:** each worktree's `target` is 12 to 16 GB, and D reached 1.9 GB free, which made a lane stop with `no space on device`. Delete the `target` of a lane once it's merged, rather than waiting for the release cleanup.

## 2026-10-31 · Phase 2: images from any path, and the OpenCode timer

- **Read the tool before trusting the plan.** The plan said to parse "Saved image: <file>" from OpenCode's tool result. OpenCode 2.2.2's npm binary holds its JavaScript as plain text, and it shows the tool returns at once, before the image exists, with a path that has no extension. A plan's description of a third-party tool is a guess until it is read.
- **OpenCode's ACP shapes:** the tool's name is only in the started `tool_call`, in `title`. The finished `tool_call_update` has no name or title, only `rawOutput.metadata {path, model, jobID}`. `path` is the stem. The completion notice has no producer in the ACP layer of 2.2.2, so the daemon watches for the file itself.
- **A title can be forged.** A shell call's title comes from the model, so a bot can title one `experimental_image_generate`. Refuse `kind:"execute"`, read only the exact structured field, and never search or parse output. A notice may finish a known call but never create a record.
- **`session.changed` doesn't update messages.** It merges summary fields and keeps the old message list (`types.ts`). To change a message in place, emit `message.completed` with its `messageId`: the client replaces the row with that id.
- **The client's turn-and-role fallback would merge notices.** Streamed text finds its row by turn and role, so a notice with a turn id could overwrite another notice from that turn. Notices now only match their own id, and notices are stored with no turn.
- **When a provider sends both a saved file and the bytes, the saved file wins.** Otherwise every image is written twice. The bytes are only the fallback, and they never reach a tool row, event or log.
- **Failure text is provider text.** Redact it and strip absolute paths before it goes in `meta` or a message, or a path reaches a phone.
- **Don't cancel on a "busy" refusal.** Sending `session/cancel` whenever a prompt fails would stop the running turn the refusal is about, while telling the owner to wait for it.
- **A test hook that changes a process-wide value needs its tests run one at a time.** Set it after the session is open, so only the call under test feels it. `applyEvent` drops a repeated sequence number, so reducer tests need increasing ones.
- **`explorer /select,` needs two arguments and backslashes:** `.arg("/select,").arg(path)`. Not yet seen opening a window in the real app.
- **`.media-image button` styles every button inside the figure.** New buttons in there need two classes to win.
- **The browser pane denies all clipboard writes, even `writeText`.** Copy can only be proven in the real app.
- **Keep an OpenCode lane's turns short while the installed app has the 30-minute limit.** Asking Gogo to report after each chunk also made review easier: each chunk's mistakes were caught before the next built on them.

## 2026-10-30 · Phase 1: modes, overrides, the sign-in read, and two lanes in parallel

- **Widening a CHECK on `agents` means rebuilding from the table's own stored SQL** (`ensure_agent_accept_edits`): it has gained columns since version 9, so a fixed column list would lose data. Change the CHECK text in the old migration SQL too, so fresh databases skip the rebuild and its backup. A CHECK it doesn't recognise returns `Ok`: opening the app matters more than one new mode. A failed backup or rebuild still returns an error.
- **Test a table rebuild on a copy of the real database before shipping**, made with SQLite's backup API so it is consistent while the app runs. Compare rows, columns, indexes, conversations, messages and settings, and run a second open to prove no second backup.
- **OpenCode's `session/prompt` had a hard 30-minute limit** (`retinue-adapter-acp/src/lib.rs`). A long turn was marked failed with "OpenCode prompt failed" while OpenCode kept working, unseen, and the next message failed in seconds because OpenCode was still busy. When a bot's status and its processes disagree, check the processes: `durationMs` close to a round number points at a timer.
- **A permission test's later "ask" cases are weak.** A pending card parks the conversation in `waiting_permission`, so later requests in the same test aren't judged by the mode. Put it back to `working` between requests.
- **Keep the project id computed ahead of `exec_options_for_session` in `new_session`,** or the project's mode applies later than launch and Claude and Codex relaunch on the first turn.
- **A default of `bypass` needs a safe fallback everywhere it is read.** The permission arm fell back to the stored default when options couldn't be read, which would have approved everything. It now falls back to `ask`.
- **Project data that is code or widens permissions must be closed at every outlet:** storage's plain `project_update` refuses `overrides`, redaction drops the key by name, project events aren't sent to devices, and backups don't carry projects.
- **On Windows, killing `cmd.exe` leaves what it started running.** Keep the child alive until `taskkill /T /F /PID` has run. Dropping it first means taskkill finds nothing and the tree survives.
- **An opt-in read must be gated where the file is opened, and tested by proving the read never ran:** an injected fetch whose counter stays 0 with the switch off and reaches 1 with it on, or the test passes on a gate that never polls. Never point a test at the real `USERPROFILE`.
- **Turning an experiment's default on breaks tests that assumed off,** spread across files. Set the switch explicitly in each one, and run the full gate rather than a filtered one. With `autoRetry` off, `after_failed_turn` returns before recording any decision.
- **No setting key has ever been renamed** (scanned 30 October 2026: every revision of `main.rs`, storage `lib.rs` and the UI's settings writes). `SETTING_SHIMS` starts empty. Repeat the scan before assuming a rename exists.
- **The bash tool can turn a typed backslash-zero into a real NUL byte,** and it did in `lib.rs`. Build such text with `chr(92)` in a script, and check `lib.rs` still has exactly one NUL. The Read tool refuses that whole file, so read it with `grep -a` or PowerShell.
- **Two lanes merged cleanly when their files were kept apart in the brief.** The one conflict was the long `validation_key` line in `settings.set`, where both lanes added to the same statement. Merge the finished lane first, and hold an unfinished fix back with `git diff -- . ':(exclude)<path>'` when its files are separate.

## 2026-10-30 · Lanes · Working with an in-app bot through the MCP

- **`.blob-dialog` wins over a new dialog's width.** `blobPage.css` loads late and sets `width: min(440px, …)`, so a one-class rule such as `.report-problem { width: … }` silently loses, and a row of buttons wraps. Write `.blob-dialog.report-problem`. Measure with `getBoundingClientRect` when a dialog looks narrow: the preview's viewport is 1280 wide even though screenshots are scaled to 800.
- **The browser pane can show a stale frame while it is hidden.** A screenshot straight after an action may predate it, and hidden pages run on slow timers, so a click at old coordinates can miss. Check state through the DOM, or wait and take the screenshot again, before calling something a bug.
- **A lane's stub tests can't prove a real agent reads context.** For a change to what the agent receives, run a throwaway daemon with its own `RETINUE_DB_PATH` and a real OpenCode free model, and plant a code word only the new path can deliver (`.retinue.json`, a note on a reply). Remove `RETINUE_HOOK_BRIDGE` and `RETINUE_REMOTE_WEB_DIR` from its environment.
- **`gh.available` only means the command runs.** Offer GitHub actions only when `login` is set too.
- **A bot's worktree folder can stay locked after its turn.** `git worktree remove --force` emptied and unregistered Sona's worktree but reported "Permission denied" on the folder itself, and no process named it on its command line. Leave the empty folder for the owner; don't kill processes to free it.
- **Check before deleting a lane worktree that holds uncommitted work.** A bot's changes stay uncommitted, so its branch shows nothing. Compare each `git status` file with `main` (ignore line endings), and check its branch has no commits of its own.
- **Stopping a Claude bot:** steering is unavailable, so use `stop_turn` then `send_message`. A message sent at once can fail with "Claude process exited" while the old process shuts down; send it again.
- **A new MCP tool needs a new session.** A connected Claude Code session keeps the tool list from when it connected. Until it reconnects, drive the bridge over stdio (`retinue-mcp.exe mcp-server --endpoint <file>`).
- **A brief must define explicit null for every optional parameter.** Phase 1A's `projectId` left it open: the front end sent null for "No project" and the daemon read it as omitted. Both sides' tests passed, and only reading the diff against the UI caught it.

## 2026-10-30 · Daemon · Phase 1A (bots across projects): what to remember

- **A conversation's project is the session DTO's `projectId`, not the bot's.** The bot's `projectId` is only a default. Context, memory and rule matching read the conversation's (`memory.rs` `session_scope`; `team::attach(st, id, agent, link, folder, project_id, options)`; resume passes the stored session's). `runtime_instructions` is test-only now; sessions use `runtime_instructions_in`.
- **`projectId` has three cases, and null is not omitted.** A string picks the project; omitted uses the default order; explicit `null` is "No project" (private workspace, unstamped, no fallback to the bot's default, which `new_session` still uses for the omitted case). Check `p.get("projectId")` for `Some(Value::Null)`, never `as_str()` alone; the front end sends null for its "No project" choice. With null plus a `projectPath`, the folder is kept and the session is unstamped unless the folder is a registered project (the read side infers it).
- **Opening order:** `team::default_conversation_project` (last project, default, private workspace). `new_session` with `projectId` overrides `projectPath`, except for a delegation in a git worktree (`link.worktree`), which keeps its worktree path and is still stamped.
- **Delegation project order** (`team::delegation_project`): `project` arg, approved `projectPath`, the sender's project, the target's default.
- **A rule is keyed by `(agent, kind, match_key, COALESCE(project_id,''))`.** Match with `project_id IS NULL OR project_id = conversation project`. Deleting a project deletes its rules. Every rule row must carry the `projectId` key (the Settings scope picker looks for it).
- **`agent_get` rejects a non-UUID id with `invalid_argument`**, not `not_found`; tests for "missing agent" need a real UUID.
- **Don't use a heredoc for Rust or Python containing backslashes** (Windows paths in test fixtures): they are halved. Use forward slashes in the fixture or the Edit tool.
- **Two daemon tests fail only inside the running Retinue app's shell:** `doctor::…database_and_hook_rows_are_one_line_and_actionable` (the app sets `RETINUE_HOOK_BRIDGE`, so the bridge is found) and `remote::…an_absent_web_folder_serves_the_built_in_page` (it sets `RETINUE_REMOTE_WEB_DIR`, so the PWA files are found). Unset both variables (`Remove-Item Env:…` in that PowerShell session only) and they pass.
- **A test fixture that registers projects at `.` makes every session "in" them** (`project_id_for_path` matches the session's folder), so the sender's project can win over a bot default; give such a test an explicit `project`.

## 2026-10-30 · Lanes · An in-app backend bot cannot build in Auto approval mode

Symptom: Sona (Claude Sonnet 5.5, medium, approval mode Auto), the Phase 1A backend lane, resumed on its own when its usage window reopened, then stopped: every `cargo` command, even `cargo --version`, came back "This command requires approval". It changed nothing, so Retinue removed its empty worktree at the end of the turn (the folder stays, empty; the `retinue/…` branch stays). Cause: Claude Code's auto mode refuses commands it cannot classify, and a headless bot has no one to ask. Fix/avoid: before giving a bot a build-and-test task, set its approval mode to **Ask** (the Director answers the cards with the MCP `answer_approval`, operator tier) or **Full access** in its worktree; the MCP cannot change a bot's mode, so the owner does it. A usage-limit pause resumes by itself (`watchdog_continuation`, "Continue where you left off"), so no message is needed after the reset. Phase 1A front end: project tags moved from the old sidebar group headers into the conversations panel's project menu (`TagEditor` is exported from `TeamSidebar.tsx`), tested in `ConversationsPanel.projects.mounted.test.tsx`.

## 2026-10-30 · ACP adapter · OpenCode effort race and the question tool

- **"OpenCode rejected the effort setting" was a race inside OpenCode, not a Retinue bug.**
  - **When:** a bot with instructions (its own, or the team instructions every bot gets when there are several) makes Retinue send model, then `mode=retinue`, then `effort`.
  - **Cause:** OpenCode's agent switch republishes the model without its variant. When that lands after the effort call, the reply echoes `default`. That happened in 3 of 4 logged runs; the evidence is in OpenCode's `packages/cli/src/acp/sessions.ts` 54–91 and `config-option.ts` 118–132.
  - **Fix:** `select_effort` in `retinue-adapter-acp/src/lib.rs` sends model and effort as one value, `provider/model/<effort>` (OpenCode's `parseModelSelection`), after the mode switch. It verifies the echo, and retries twice 200 ms apart.
  - **Failure reasons** now carry the provider's error text, or "answered X instead of Y".
