- **Fix:** all look stylesheets are static imports in `apps/desktop/src/main.tsx`; `src/ui/themeBuild.test.ts` forbids dynamic imports of look CSS (`098831e`, merged in `c119f93`, shipped in v0.1.0-beta.36). Since 20 October 2026 the only look stylesheet is `ui/themes/frost.css`; the Dot and Pop sheets named above were removed in `5eb6879`.
- **Avoid:** never put several `import()` calls in one conditional expression; give each its own arrow function (as `ui/fonts.ts` does). Check bundling-sensitive changes in `npm run build` output or the installed app, not only the preview.

## 2026-10-19 · Process · A lane's root cause can be wrong while its fix works
FIX42's lane blamed "lazy CSS does not load in the WebView" (contradicted by Legacy's lazy CSS working for many betas). Always test the stated cause against evidence before recording it.

## 2026-10-19 · Toolchain · Rust 1.94 on the build machine
`AtomicU64::try_update` is unstable on 1.94 (fixed with `fetch_update`, `28da243`); clippy on 1.94 flags `!x.is_some_and(...)` patterns (use `is_none_or`), needless borrows and items after test modules (`7412a9c`). Earlier betas were built with a newer toolchain. Run clippy before every release.

## 2026-10-19 · Native build · Apostrophe in the checkout path
`cargo check/test/build -p retinue-desktop` fails with `RC2135 file not found: D:\Basem\'s\...\retinue.ico` from the main checkout. Build the desktop crate and releases from a short path (`D:\fxNN`, `D:\bNN`).

## 2026-10-19 · Lanes · OpenCode 2 flags
OpenCode 2.0 removed `--variant` and `--dir`; effort is `-m provider/model#high`, run from the worktree, add `--auto`. Old flags print help and exit 1. Upgrading OpenCode while a lane runs killed two runs (exit code 9).

## 2026-10-19 · Lanes · Codex sandbox limits
No network, no writes outside the workspace: npm installs fail, and Vite/Vitest cannot write their temp config through a `node_modules` junction (`EPERM`; use `--configLoader runner`). The Director installs packages and runs the full gates.

## 2026-10-19 · Tests · Load-induced failures
Full Vitest runs alongside cargo builds or lanes produced 57 and 100 false failures (timeouts). Re-run failing files alone before treating them as real.

## 2026-10-19 · Tests · Settings broadcast queue
A daemon test that sets many settings must assert the broadcast events it cares about before setting more keys: every `settings.set` queues a `settings.changed` event, and later `events.recv()` calls read the queued ones first (FIX40 `appearance_settings_validate_persist_and_broadcast`).

## 2026-10-19 · Design · Selected rows in filled looks
Dot and Pop were removed on 20 October 2026, but the rule stands for any filled look. Dot (inverted) and Pop (butter) selections both shipped with unreadable descendants at first (white title on white; cream role chip and amber status on butter) because base rules for `.role-chip`, row times and `.bot-row-preview.waiting` are more specific. Scope the overrides under the selected element with enough specificity and check every descendant in dark and light.

## 2026-10-19 · Design · Frost glass level
At tint .84 the Lake wallpaper's bright band bled through all panels as a stripe; Solid is tint .93 / blur 24 px. The owner wants Solid as the default and no glow anywhere.

## 2026-10-19 · CSS · Mini avatars
`ArtFrame` marks small avatars with `data-mini`, not a class; style them with `.art-frame[data-mini] ...`.

## 2026-10-19 · Daemon · Look and settings keys are allowlisted
`valid_look` rejects unknown top-level look keys, and `normalizeLook`, `agentLook`, `lookKey` rebuild looks from known keys only: a new look field must be added in both TypeScript and `crates/retinue-storage/src/lib.rs`, or it is silently dropped or rejected. Haze's `icon` key (null or one of `logo`, `logo-tint`, `logo-mono`, `px-robot` … `px-bolt`, `ICONS` in `valid_look`) is the latest example: it also needs the Manager descriptions in `team.rs` and `proposals.rs`.

## 2026-10-19 · Links · Safe opener
Chat links are `<a href target="_blank">` routed through `openSafeLink`; middle-click and Ctrl/Cmd/Shift-click must also go through it (`onAuxClick`), and the desktop app must never fall back to `window.open` inside the webview.

## 2026-10-19 · Release · SHA256SUMS
`scripts/release-windows.ps1` does not create `SHA256SUMS.txt`; upload it separately (format `<sha256>  <installer>` and a newline) and verify its digest.

## 2026-10-24 · Preview · Portals into a React-owned node
Portaling tooltips and drag ghosts into the page's root `.mini` element (rendered by React) threw `removeChild ... not a child of this node` on unmount. Portal into an empty host element that React renders but never fills (`<div id="mini-portal" />`) and keep it inside the themed root so its CSS variables still apply. A page that is both a Vite entry and edited live also re-runs `createRoot` on hot reload; keep the root on the container (`host.miniRoot ??= createRoot(host)`).

## 2026-10-24 · CSS · Lifting content above a background layer broke absolutely placed children
- **Symptom:** in Haze the side-conversation pill shrank to 23 px around one logo, and the companion focus card's avatar slid onto the name.
- **Root cause:** `.chat-header > :not(.haze-aura) { position: relative; z-index: 1 }` (and the same on `.island-card.focus`) made `.chat-title` the containing block of the absolutely centred `.side-header`, and overrode `.card-bot`'s `position: absolute`.
- **Fix:** keep the children as they are; give the parent `isolation: isolate` and the background layer `z-index: -1`.
- **Avoid:** never re-position a component's children from a theme to stack them; push the decoration down instead. Likewise, a theme token used as a page background (`--surface-panel`, `--surface-app`) is also used by popovers and ring cut-outs: making it transparent made the profile menu see-through and the usage rings pies (rings now cut their centre with a mask).

## 2026-10-25 · Composer · Model confirmation and effort / attachment geometry
- **Model confirmation (FIX44):** the dialog is now tied to real context loss, not to “any changed model”. `main.rs:update_session_model` requires `confirmModelChange` only when the conversation has user messages, a delivered provider context (`session_had_provider_turns`) or a provider session on record (`providerSessionId`, `contextUsed`, `contextModel`); otherwise a first choice in an empty chat applies silently. The composer mirrors this in `modelChangeNeedsWarning` (`SessionExecutionControls.tsx`) and also reacts to a `confirmation_required` refusal by showing the same dialog, so the daemon contract still holds. A model change now writes `session.model.update` **before** `agent.update`, so cancelling leaves both the conversation and the bot untouched; effort and speed changes keep the old bot-first order that the open picker relies on.
- **Geometry (FIX44):** `.composer-attachment-remove` is a 24px box with `padding: 0`, `place-items: center` and a `--focus-ring` on `:focus-visible`. `.session-effort-popover` is `width: max-content` with `min-width: min(320px, calc(100vw - 24px))` and `max-width: min(460px, calc(100vw - 24px))`; labels are `min-width: max-content` in a wrapping flex row, so they are never ellipsized and a narrow window wraps them. The old 320/404px fixed widths and the ellipsis on `.session-effort-value` are gone.
- **Sent chat images (FIX44):** `session.prompt` copies each accepted image into `<data dir>/message-images/<turn>-<n>.<ext>` and records `file` on the message (older messages keep only `path`). The new `message.image` RPC takes `{sessionId, messageId, index}` — never a path — checks the message belongs to the conversation, re-checks the image signature, and returns a data URL; a missing/damaged file is `not_found`, which the UI renders as a placeholder. Deleting a conversation removes its copied files. `MessageAttachments` shows thumbnails with a viewer (Escape/backdrop close, focus returns); without a session/message identity it falls back to the old filename chip.
- **Steer queue (FIX44):** the queue banner and the steer/queue line moved inside `.composer-box` as `ComposerStatus` (`PromptQueue.tsx`): one 22px row that always exists, a count chip that unfolds the rows, and paused/retry/queue-reason text in that same row. Do not put the queue banner back outside the card; it stacks and crowds the composer.
- **Saved chats and titles (FIX44):** `experiments::title_from_prompt` now strips fenced code and code-looking first lines, drops leading greetings/openers ("hey, can you fix the login bug" → "fix the login bug"), and falls back to `Shared N images`, `Code review`, `Quick hello` or `Quick chat` for prompts that name nothing. The storage write still refuses a renamed row, a side conversation and any conversation with a prior user message.
- **Permission reversion (FIX44):** `permissionWrite` (`ComposerControls.tsx`) writes `null` only when the choice *is* the bot's default, and **always** writes `bypass` for Full access. Writing `null` for bypass cleared the bot's setting whenever the bot already defaulted to bypass, so the conversation fell back to the global Ask. A model lock's permission pin still applies from the next turn only. Separately, the snapshot's agents (`Storage::snapshot` → `agent_rows_locked`) did not select `approval_mode`, so after a reload the composer showed the global default even though the bot was bypass; the snapshot now carries `approvalMode` and `effectiveApprovalMode`. The UI never calls `permissions.policy.get`, so the snapshot is the only source.
- **A reload mounts before the daemon answers.** `message.image` is now in `retry_startup_read` (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`), and `MessageImage` retries a "daemon is not running" refusal (not a `not_found`) before showing its placeholder. Any new read RPC that the transcript calls on mount needs both, or a reload shows a permanent placeholder.
- **Avoid:** when diagnosing a “new chat” warning, check the session id/persisted-vs-draft state and message count separately. For picker geometry, inspect computed button padding as well as grid alignment.

## 2026-10-26 · Task dragging and details

The board's internal task drag must use pointer capture, a movement threshold and elementFromPoint hit-testing against data-agent-id lanes. WebView2 native file drag handling can intercept the HTML drag path; do not disable native file drops globally to fix an internal board gesture. A drop still uses the existing connected/busy checks and daemon dispatch; suppress the task-open click only after a drag. Plain clicks and the Assign menu remain available.

Task detail dialogs close on a direct backdrop pointer-down. Only the detail body scrolls; header/actions stay visible. Render brief/goal/report via SafeMarkdown and openSafeLink rather than raw paragraphs. Keep Retry left, Close/Open conversation and state actions right, with matching control radii. The tree/timeline structure is unchanged. Owner requested rapid edits without a test sweep.

## 2026-10-26 · Native · A see-through look needs a transparent native window, not only transparent CSS (Haze, uncommitted)

Symptom: Haze's background could not be made transparent in CSS alone. Cause: the main window was created without `transparent` (`tauri.conf.json`), so WebView2 painted an opaque default background behind the page. Fix: `"transparent": true` on the main window (tao then applies the same empty DWM blur region as the companion window, wry sets the WebView2 default background to alpha 0), plus an opaque base for the looks that must not be see-through (Frost body colour under its wallpaper; Stone's `--surface-app` is already opaque). It is a creation-time property: a running window needs a rebuild and restart. Not verified in a running native window (the owner forbade tests and screenshots in that wave).

## 2026-10-26 (T3 wave)

- **A second dev app must not share the identifier.** `tauri-plugin-single-instance` is keyed by the bundle identifier, so `scripts/dev-windows.ps1`'s `com.retinue.desktop.dev` can fold into an already running review build. The T3 preview uses `com.retinue.desktop.t3dev` and Vite ports 1440 to 1459, with its own `RETINUE_DB_PATH`.
- **Media and CSP.** `img-src` already allowed `data:`/`blob:`; audio and video needed `media-src 'self' blob:`. `fetch(dataUrl)` is blocked (`connect-src` has no `data:`), so decode base64 yourself into a Blob. WebView2's PDF viewer does not run in our CSP or inside a sandboxed iframe, so PDFs are a card, not an inline view.
- **A delegation turn can end before its row is "running".** `team::after_turn` runs in its own task, so a fast turn can finish before `delegations::started`; `started` must read the turn's stored state back and finish the row itself.
- **Rich tables must keep the author's text.** Converting "12%" or "$1,200" to a number for display drops the unit; keep the cell text and read the number only to sort or plot.
- **Heredocs and regex backslashes.** Writing a Node patch script through an unquoted shell heredoc strips backslashes in regexes (`\s` becomes `s`); write the script with the file tool, then run it.
- **node_modules is shared through the worktree junctions.** Adding a package: run `npm install <pkg> --workspace @retinue/desktop --package-lock-only` in one worktree, then put the package into the main `node_modules` (or run `npm install` in main); the lanes' junctions see it at once. A lane worktree the Director created may have **no** junction at all (17 November 2026: `tsc` not found), and `git status` will not say so: `cmd /c mklink /J "<lane>\node_modules" "<any lane>\node_modules"` makes one in a second, and `npm run typecheck` and `npx vitest run` then work.
- **Two spawn sites can close a future-type cycle (E0391).** A request hook in `forward_events` that awaited `deliver` made `session_prompt_impl` depend on itself; a synchronous `spawn_update` (a plain `fn` that spawns) breaks the cycle. Spawning an `async` block that awaits the delivery path does not.
- **Do not type-check tests when the owner says no tests.** `apps/desktop/tsconfig.json` includes the test files, so `tsc -p apps/desktop`, `tsc -b` and `npm run build` all compile them. For a no-tests wave use a temporary tsconfig that extends it and excludes `*.test.ts(x)` (`apps/desktop/tsconfig.prod.tmp.json`, untracked), and `npx vite build` for the bundle.
- **A PDF viewer needs three things in a Tauri CSP.** The worker must be your own bundled file (`...worker.min.mjs?url`), the viewer must draw to a canvas (no iframe), and `isEvalSupported` must be false. WebView2's built-in PDF viewer is not available in our setup.
- **A question holds its assignment open.** A side conversation's turn that ends with a pending `ask_user` card is not a result: `delegations::after_turn` returns `TurnEnd::Waiting` (no finish, no reply claim, no relay) and the answer's turn is adopted by the same running row. Anything that finishes a side assignment (cancel, fail, interrupt, restart) must also close that session's pending cards without prompting the child. The side-conversation gate in `session_prompt_impl` has to allow `PromptOrigin::QuestionAnswer`, or the answer is refused `read_only` and pauses the queue.

## 2026-10-27 · Native · Haze frosted glass needs the OS compositor; CSS cannot blur the desktop (branch haze/glass, uncommitted)

Symptom/ask: "Haze should be frosted glass", but the 26 October fix only made the page 80% opaque over a transparent window, which shows the desktop sharp, not blurred. Cause: WebView2 composes only its own page, so `backdrop-filter` can never blur what is behind the window; only DWM can. Fix: `src-tauri/src/backdrop.rs` calls `window-vibrancy` directly (Tauri's `set_effects` swallows errors, so it cannot report a refusal): build 22523+ `DWMSBT_TRANSIENTWINDOW` (tint colour ignored), 17763+ composition acrylic with a tint, then `apply_blur`. `ui/windowBackdrop.ts` watches `data-style` and `data-theme` and sets `<html data-backdrop>` only when the blur is really drawn, and `haze.css` then turns `.haze-backdrop` into a thin veil plus the blobs over it (never an opaque layer); without it the opaque 80% backdrop remains the fallback. Details that bit or could bite: the system acrylic follows the window's immersive dark mode, so the shell forces `set_theme(Dark|Light)` to match the app and `set_theme(None)` when leaving Haze (window-level `set_theme` only touches the title bar, not WebView2's colour scheme); Windows' Transparency effects switch makes acrylic a flat colour while the call still succeeds, so read the `EnableTransparency` value under `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Themes\Personalize`; first paint needs both `backdrop.json` re-applied in `setup` and `retinue.backdrop` in localStorage read by `index.html`. A fresh worktree's `cargo check -p retinue-desktop` needs the gitignored sidecars in `src-tauri/binaries/` and `apps/desktop/dist/` (`npm run build -w apps/desktop`); `--lib` avoids neither. **Not verified at runtime** (owner rule: no screenshots or tests): that the transparent decorated WebView2 window shows the backdrop, the density, and Windows 10 resize lag. Isolated dev window: `D:\hz1\target\lane\run-hzdev.ps1` (identifier `com.retinue.desktop.hzglass`, ports 1470 to 1489).

## 2026-10-31 · Machines · What Phase 4 taught

- **Compare host names without regard to case.** Native's certificate names the machine by `COMPUTERNAME`, which is upper case, and every client sends it lower case, so a link by host name was refused with `forbidden_origin` until `web.rs` used `eq_ignore_ascii_case`.
- **`st.emit` persists events.** Anything that must stay in memory can't ride in an event payload. `machine.changed` first carried the other machine's bot names into the event log; it now carries only `{machineId, state}`.
- **A value read once goes stale when the other side owns it.** The link's tier was only read at pairing, so raising it on the other computer never unlocked Take over. `refresh_one` now reads `GET /api/me`.
- **A read that fails is not evidence.** `repo_identity::Origin::Unknown` keeps the stored identity. Only a finished git run clears it. Use the same shape for any derived column.
- **A security-relevant field needs one writer.** `trustedSharedHash` could be set through `project.update`, skipping the confirm step. The public validator now refuses it, a private one reads stored rows, and one storage method writes it.
- **`std::fs::write` follows a planted link.** A repository can hold a symlink at a temp path you're about to write. Use `create_new`, and on `AlreadyExists` remove that path and retry once.
- **`FETCH_HEAD` is per worktree.** A fast-forward inside a linked worktree must name the fetched commit by id.
- **Any `Command::output()` on a network command needs a real limit.** A fetch can hang. `worktree_attach::run_limited` polls `try_wait`, reads stdout on its own thread, and kills the tree with `taskkill /T` at the limit.
- **Never let an import overwrite a live row.** An id from another machine can match a conversation here, for example after copying a database. Only a row whose `movedTo` names the giving machine may be updated.
- **A dialog opened from inside a dialog needs a portal.** `.blob-dialog` has a transform and a `backdrop-filter`, so a nested fixed backdrop is trapped inside it. `TakeOverDialog` uses `createPortal`.
- **A long reading surface can't be Haze glass.** `--surface-overlay` is translucent, and text behind a transcript collides with it. `.machine-view` mixes it with `Canvas`.

## 2026-10-31 · Phases 5 to 7 · What they taught

- **A per-bot environment is also a process key.** `options.env` is hashed into the shared-process key by the Codex and ACP adapters, and Claude spawns per session. So a per-bot variable must join `ExecOptions.env` at `main.rs`, not the child's inherited environment, or two bots silently share the first bot's process.
- **A provider's ACP flag isn't always the `acp` subcommand.** Gemini CLI and Qwen Code take `--acp`. `adapter_for` returns `None` for an unknown provider, so a provider that's listed but runs nothing fails there: check it first.
- **`codex app-server generate-json-schema` needs `--experimental`** to match what the adapter negotiates. `core.autocrlf` rewrites pinned JSON on checkout, hence `schema/.gitattributes`.
- **Who is signed in:** `claude auth status` is JSON. `codex login status` only says "Logged in using ChatGPT", so use the app server's `account/read`, skipping lines with no `id`. `opencode auth list` names no person, and its `label` is the credential kind. Never run `opencode auth export`: it prints secrets.
- **Web Push:** the `aes128gcm` additional data is empty (RFC 8188 section 2.1), not the header. `hkdf`'s `expand` appends the `0x01` counter itself. `serde_json::json!` sorts keys, so build VAPID segments by hand.
- **`.retinue.json` already had a field.** `instructions` shipped in beta.43, so new code must keep unknown keys when writing and accept a file with no `version`. Read the release notes for a file's history before writing a format for it.
- **OpenCode lanes share a process.** When one turn hit the installed app's 30-minute limit, the other OpenCode turns ended seconds later, with no error of their own. Their files were fine. Keep OpenCode turns short on any build before beta.44, and resend to all of them.
- **The app runs four bot conversations at once** (`concurrency.botsAtOnce`, 1 to 16, the owner's setting). A send over that returns an error saying it will start later, and it does not start. Check a lane really started.
- **A release script must stop on a failed test.** `release_b44` carried on after UI timeouts under load. They were harmless, but the script couldn't know that.
- **Tests that make real requests need a limit that survives a busy machine.** The everyday 10-second client limit failed handoff tests while lanes compiled. Use a long limit in tests, and don't count events exactly while a background loop runs.

## 2026-11-01 · Usage · A `quota.updated` event with no snapshot loses its reason

- **Symptom:** Claude and OpenCode Go read "Unavailable" in the usage panel while Codex shows its meters, and the panel offers no way to turn reading on.
- **Cause:** the daemon's row is honest (`snapshot: null`, `lastError: "read_off"`), but `applyEvent` in `apps/desktop/src/types.ts` builds the fallback row for an event whose `quota.snapshot` is not an object, and that fallback hard-codes `lastError: null` with `snapshot: quota`. `quotaModel.ts` then has no windows and no error, so it says `Unavailable`, and `QuotaBar.tsx`'s "Turn on" (which needs `readOff`) never renders. Codex is fine because its snapshot is an object and takes the other branch.
- **Why it hides:** `quota.list` is fetched once per connection (`App.tsx`), and the honest row lives only milliseconds; every poll (5 minutes, `runtime.changed`, and each `read_off` persist) rewrites it through the event path.
- **Check the DB, not the UI:** `quota_snapshots` in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Retinue\retinue.db` holds `last_error` per provider (`read_off`, `no_credentials`, `unavailable`, …). A missing `usage.readCliSignIns` row means the owner never allowed the read, which is the default.
- **Rule:** an event reducer must carry every field the list row carries. When a payload nests a nullable snapshot, keep `lastError`, `failureCount` and `retryAfter` rather than inventing a fresh row.

## 2026-11-03: opening a conversation showed "now" (updated_at bumped by housekeeping)

- **Symptom:** clicking a conversation made its row read "now" though nothing was sent.
- **Cause:** opening warms the provider (`session.warm` → resume in `main.rs`). The resume called `update_session_state(.., "idle")` (and `clear_session_provider_id`), and both write `updated_at`, which the sidebar shows as the conversation's age.
- **Fix:** `Storage::set_session_state_quietly` (no `updated_at`) for the resume path; `clear_session_provider_id` no longer touches `updated_at`.
- **Avoid:** `updated_at` means "something happened in the conversation". Housekeeping (resume, clearing a provider id) must not write it.

## 2026-11-08 · Native (GPUI) fix round 2 · The window quit without a word

- **A panic in a GPUI window looks like a silent quit.** `native.log` ended with "daemon child stopped" and no "exited": the panic unwound through `Root`'s drop (stopping the daemon), then crossed an `extern "C"` boundary and aborted, so `main` never reached its last log line and the message went to a stderr nobody watches. Install a panic hook as the **first** thing in `main` (`trace::install_panic_hook`): it writes `PANIC at <file>:<line>:<col>: <message>` and a `std::backtrace::Backtrace::force_capture()` (which ignores `RUST_BACKTRACE`, so a debug build always has frames).
- **The cause was a GPUI double lease:** "cannot update Root while it is already being updated". `Entity::lease` *removes* the entity from the map while it is leased, so `entity.update`/`entity.read` on the same entity from inside its own listener or update panics, and the panic aborts the process.
  - The reported sequence reached it through `Ctrl+K`: the action listener holds `Root`, `toggle_quick` set the quick-switcher field's text, and the field's change hook called `root.update(..)` inside that.
  - The same shape was waiting in the composer: a keystroke's change hook read `self.composer`, Enter's submit handler cleared it, and Up/the `/command` menu updated it — all while the field was leased.
  - **Fix:** text-field hooks take the field itself (`Fn(&mut TextInput, ..)`, `Fn(&str, usize, ..)` for changes) and edit *it*; anything that needs the window goes through `Root`, which is a different entity. Programmatic `set_text`/`clear` do not fire the change hook at all. A listener that holds `Root` and needs to edit the field uses `Context::update_entity(&field, |input, cx| ..)`.
- **An overlay drawn by taking it out of the window disappears on the next repaint.** `render_menu_layer` did `self.menu.take()`, so the menu existed for exactly one frame; this window repaints every frame (marquee, working shimmer), which is why "…" never showed a popover. Overlays are drawn from `self.menu.clone()`.
- **`w_full()`/`min_w(0)` inside a content-sized parent is a percentage of nothing.** The user bubble is as wide as its text (up to its cap), so the inline-text block inside it collapsed to one character and a long message became a single column of letters down the whole pane. A bubble draws the shaped text itself (`styled_inline`); a block is only for parents whose width is settled.
- **Moving a transparent window programmatically leaves a strip of the old frame at its old edge.** Every "vertical band" screenshot taken after a `SetWindowPos` move had one; the same window started in place has none. Move it before it paints, or don't move it, when capturing.
- **A row answers the *press*, and over its whole area.** Two ways a card or a row can look alive and do nothing: the click sat on the inner block that holds the title, so a press on the card's own padding (which is drawn as the card and lights up on hover) was outside it; and `on_click` only fires when the same element is under the pointer at press *and* release, which a list that re-sorts, a fold that animates or a marquee that starts can break between the two. Rows that select or open (conversation cards, tree rows, the tree's conversation cards, the roster) bind `on_mouse_down`, and a button inside such a row (`…`) binds its own press and `stop_propagation`s it.
- **Guard the classes, not just the case:** `ui::guards` scans the source for the three shapes (a hook touching its own field, a method handed the box reaching the entity, an overlay taken instead of cloned, a bubble given a block).

## 2026-11-08 · Native (GPUI) fix round · What the first screenshot pass caught

- **A file chip must never start a program.** `explorer.exe <path>` on a path a bot wrote runs whatever it wrote: a `.bat`, `.ps1`, `.lnk` or `.exe` is one click away from executing. The React app only *previews* and says why; the native window now does the same (image or text inside the window, `explorer.exe /select,<canonical path>` to highlight it) and `open_with_default_app` is gone. A source-scanning test (`files::spawn_guard`) fails if any new call site starts a program outside the three that may (the daemon, `reg add` for the notification id, and that one `/select,`).
- **A `list` row keeps the height it was last measured at.** A row that grows (streamed text, a tool finishing) is then drawn at its old height and the next row is painted over it. `ListState::remeasure()` after any event that can change a row's content, after a `reset`/`splice`, and whenever `viewport_bounds().size.width` changes, is the browser's relayout.
- **`min-width: auto` is the default on flex items in GPUI too.** A paragraph with an unbreakable token (inline code with no spaces) expands every ancestor and the pane clips it. The chain needs `w_full().min_w(px(0.0))` at each level, and a list row needs `flex_1().min_w(px(0.0))` around the text beside its marker.
- **Inline-code backgrounds need a line height with room.** A tight line height paints the highlight over the line above, which the light theme shows first: `line_height(size * 1.65)` on the text div.
- **An action only arrives if something is focused.** `on_key_down` on the root view is not a window listener: with no field in the tree (the Settings page) the event goes nowhere. Bind the shortcuts as `actions!` with `Some("Workspace")`, put `.track_focus(..).key_context("Workspace")` on the root, and focus the root when a page has no field.
- **A backdrop that closes on mouse-down swallows what is inside it.** Mouse handlers propagate to lower z-index handlers by default, so a dialog card or a menu panel must `cx.stop_propagation()` on its own press, or it closes before its item's click arrives. The "…" button of a card does the same so it does not also open the conversation.
- **An absolutely positioned "…" over a title covers the title.** Give it a row of its own (`[open area | button]`), which is also what the React column does, and the title truncates in the width that is left.

## 2026-11-08 · Native (GPUI) slice 3 · What this slice cost to learn

- **A `null` where a list is expected throws the whole snapshot away.** The live check printed only `snapshot did not parse: invalid type: null, expected a sequence` and the window showed nothing: the daemon writes `null` for an empty `quotas` and for a message's `attachments`. `#[serde(default)]` does not accept an explicit `null`; every list on the DTOs now uses `deserialize_with = "serde_defaults::null_as_default"`. A new list field needs it, and the check is "one null anywhere makes the window empty".
- **`anchored()`/`deferred()` are in `crates/gpui/src/elements/`, not the top-level `src/`.** Grepping only `src/*.rs` says they do not exist at the pinned rev; they do, and they are what makes a popover survive a scroll container.
- **A menu cannot ask GPUI where its trigger is.** `ClickEvent::position()` is the window's pointer position, so a menu opens at the click: the same code serves a "…" button, a right-click and the project "+".
- **`ClipboardEntry::Image` is a BMP on Windows when the source app offered only a DIB.** GPUI's reader wraps the DIB in a BMP header, so pasted screenshots must be re-encoded to PNG before the daemon sees them. `src-tauri` escapes this by reading through `arboard`; do not copy that here, because arboard 3.6.1 needs `clipboard-win 5.4.1`, which is yanked, and an offline build refuses it.
- **A `session.model.update` with `model: null` is refused**, so a conversation's model can never go back to "the bot's default": the model menu offers concrete models only.
- **`clientMessageId` has a shape** (8 to 80 characters of letters, digits, hyphens or underscores) and is what makes a resend after a timeout return the first message instead of sending twice.
- **A copy of an 804 MB installed database takes ~20 s to answer the bootstrap line** (it re-creates the read indexes); a 10 s window limit made every start look like a failure. The limit is 45 s now.
- **A daemon killed rather than closed leaves the pending cards deleted on the next start.** A fabricated `permission_requests` row does not survive startup, so an approval card cannot be exercised by seeding the database; it needs a real waiting request.
- **Test the live path against a real snapshot, not an empty one.** The empty database start passed every gate and hid the null-list bug for an hour.

## 2026-11-08 · Native (GPUI) slice 2 · What the review caught

- **Replacing a SQLite file:** when the daemon has been killed rather than closed, `retinue.db-wal` and `-shm` can survive. Moving only `retinue.db` aside and writing a fresh copy in its place lets SQLite replay the stale log onto the new file. Move the sidecars with the main file (`install_copy` in `apps/native/src/ui/root.rs`), and run a large copy off the UI thread.
- **GPUI flex scroll areas** need `.min_h(px(0.0))`, or the list grows past its parent and runs over a fixed footer (the CSS `min-height: 0` rule).
- **Clipped text in GPUI** shows no "…" unless the element uses `.truncate()`. `overflow_hidden` with `nowrap` only cuts. A fixed-height multi-line box needs an explicit `line_height`, or the second line is cut in half.
- **Checking the native window:** the lane can't see it. The Director launches the packaged exe with `RETINUE_DB_PATH` set to a backup copy of the real database, clicks with `SetCursorPos`/`mouse_event`, and captures with `CopyFromScreen`. Every fix round above came from those screenshots, not from the tests.

## 2026-11-08 · Native (GPUI) slice 5 · A floating window and one daemon connection

- **A Win32 window call inside a paint re-enters GPUI.** `ShowWindow`/`SetWindowPos` pump messages, the window procedure calls back into the app while it is borrowed, and the process dies with `RefCell already borrowed` (`app/async_context.rs`). Defer them to the background executor, as GPUI's own `Window::resize` does; reads (`GetWindowRect`, `GetCursorPos`, `GetDpiForWindow`, `EnumDisplayMonitors`) are safe inline. The companion's first version crashed on its first paint for exactly this.
- **`Window::window_handle` shadows the trait.** The inherent method returns GPUI's own handle; the raw `HWND` needs `<gpui::Window as HasWindowHandle>::window_handle(window)`, and `WindowKind::PopUp` is what makes that window frameless and absent from the taskbar.
- **A hotkey belongs to its registering thread.** `RegisterHotKey(NULL, …)` posts `WM_HOTKEY` to the caller's queue, so a dedicated thread with `GetMessageW` and no window serves both shortcuts; a refused one returns 0 and the app must say the React app's own sentence rather than fail. On a machine with Retinue running, both shortcuts are already gone.
- **Never edit source in bulk with `Set-Content -NoNewline`.** Handed an array it joins with no separators: twenty new files became one-line comments and only the compiler noticed. Write files with the edit tool, and when a script must touch them, pass a single string (`-Raw` in, `-Raw` out).

## 2026-11-08 · Native (GPUI) slice 4 · Tasks, schedules, the rail, diffs and a terminal

- **`files::spawn_guard` blocks `taskkill`, so a terminal tab cannot take its whole process tree down.** The installed app's pty kills with `taskkill /T /F`; here that is a program started outside the three allowed call sites, and the guard fails the build. `portable_pty::Child::kill` ends the shell, a command the shell started can outlive it, and a job object (kill-on-close) is the way to do better.
- **A `const` array gives no `&'static` reference.** `&TEMPLATES[i]` on a `const` is a temporary; a closure that keeps the reference needs `pub static`. The schedule editor's "start from an idea" did.
- **`gpui` does not re-export taffy's `Position`.** A custom element that must overlay its parent needs `Style { position: Absolute, inset }` and cannot name the enum, so the terminal's grid size comes from `window.bounds()` minus the fixed column widths instead of from a measured element.
- **`Rgba` has no `opacity`; `Hsla` does.** A tinted diff row is `Rgba::from(Hsla::from(color).opacity(0.14))`, and `.bg()` takes either through `Background`, but a `match` arm that returns one and then the other will not type-check.
- **A slice's state belongs in one struct per area, with the root's hooks one line each.** Five fields on `Root` (`tasks`, `schedules`, `changes`, `terminal`, `delegations`) and five one-line hooks in `root.rs` kept the Director's restyling of `sidebar.rs`, `conversations.rs` and `conversation.rs` untouched.
- **A test expectation about a terminal is worth checking against what a terminal really does.** Backspace moves the caret and does not erase; a 3-row screen shrunk to 2 rows keeps the first two, not the last; a 4-row header block is four rows.

## 2026-11-09 · Rebrand applied to main · What the merge taught

- **A lane that renames a whole tree must start from main's tip.** The rebrand lane started at the last pushed commit, ten commits behind main, so its diff could not be merged. What worked: rerun the lane's rule script (`scripts/rebrand.py`) on main, then apply only the lane's hand fixes. Get those by running the script on the lane's own base and diffing that against the lane's result.
- **"Leave image file names alone" left the old name in code.** The icon, tray and brand paths, and throwaway temp file names in tests, kept old names. A follow-up renamed every old-named image with `git mv` and rewrote references, full paths first and bare names second. A bare-name swap can point a path at the wrong folder (`assets/retinue-tile-dark-128.png` instead of `assets/brand/…`), so build every crate that does `include_bytes!` an image after such a pass.

## 2026-11-09 · Polish batch 1 · What it taught

- **Opening a conversation woke it up.** Symptom: clicking a conversation started it working ("Picking up where it left off", "Continuing automatically") and its sidebar time jumped to now. Cause: selecting a conversation calls `session.warm` (`App.tsx`, 400 ms after selection), which ran `activate_session(Owner::Warm)`, and that called `retry::continue_on_activation`, enqueueing "Continue where you left off." for any conversation with `continuation_pending=1` (every conversation that was working when Retinue closed: `recover_after_restart`). Separately, every state change bumps `sessions.updated_at`, and the sidebar showed and sorted by it. Fix: only an explicit `session.resume` (`Owner::Rpc`) continues; warming never does (`main.rs` `activate_session`, test `p8_killed_turn_continues_on_activation_with_recap_after_resume_failure`). Sessions now carry `lastMessageAt` (last user/assistant message), and the sidebar, conversation list and companion picker show and sort by it.
- **A new npm dependency in the running preview can load React twice** ("Invalid hook call", `useRef` of null in the new package). Vite's dependency cache was optimised before the install. Restart the preview server (`preview_stop` then `preview_start`); a page reload is not enough.
- **OpenUI (`@openuidev/react-lang` 1.1.0, MIT) runs no model code**: no `eval`/`new Function`; its only network call is opt-in PostHog telemetry from `library.prompt()` in Node/Bun/Deno (needs `OPENUI_RUNTIME_TELEMETRY_ENABLED`), never in a browser window. Its form state arrives as `{ [formName]: { field: { value } } }`; flatten it (`ui/openui/OpenUiBlock.tsx` `replyText`).
- **Lane worktrees export with `git add -A -N` then `git diff --binary`.** `git apply --3way` refuses when the main checkout has unstaged edits to the same file ("does not match index") and applies nothing; plain `git apply --reject` merges and leaves `.rej` files for real conflicts.
- **Daemon tests that call `dispatch` many times can overflow the 2 MiB test stack**; run the daemon suite with `RUST_MIN_STACK=33554432`.
- **A new agent column must be added to all three agent JSON selects in storage** (`agent_read` among them), or the field silently disappears from some paths. Tests that install library skills must set `st.team.data_dir`. For quick daemon checks skip the slow git tests: `-- --skip machines_tests --skip handoff_tests`.
- **The docs library only reads tracked Markdown** (`git ls-files`), so untracking files (the native app, 9 Nov 2026) needs `node scripts/docs-library.mjs build` before `check` passes.

## 2026-11-10 · SmartScreen and Defender block the installer

- **Symptom:** downloading the beta.51 installer from GitHub was blocked with no "Run anyway". **Cause:** the installer ships unsigned (the SignPath secret and variables are not set, so `release.yml` skips signing). Defender's cloud ML flagged it as `Program:Win32/Contebrew.A!ml` (a false positive; see `Get-MpThreat`/`Get-MpThreatDetection`), and Smart App Control (`HKLM\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Control\CI\Policy` `VerifiedAndReputablePolicyState` = 1) blocks every unsigned, unknown app outright. **Fix:** submit each release at microsoft.com/wdsi/filesubmission as a developer false positive, and finish code signing (`docs/operations/code-signing.md`); only a trusted signature gets past Smart App Control.
- **A whole-tree rename can turn a migration into self-harm.** The FIX11 NSIS pre-install hook looked for the *old* product's uninstall key; the rebrand rewrote that name to `Retinue`, so it killed and silently uninstalled the current app on every manual reinstall. It was removed (`installer-hooks.nsh`, `tauri.conf.json` `installerHooks`). After a rename, grep for code that targets the old name on purpose (migrations, registry keys, folders) and check it still does.

## 2026-11-10 · Haze onboarding on a white window

- **Symptom:** the first-run "Choose your look" screen in the installed app showed near-white text on a white window once Haze was picked. **Cause:** Haze sets `--surface-app: transparent` and relies on `<AppWallpaper />` (`.haze-backdrop`), but `App.tsx` returned `<LaunchIntro>` and `<FirstRunOnboarding>` early, before the fragment that renders the wallpaper, so those screens sat on the bare transparent WebView. The browser preview hid it: `launch-preview.html` didn't import `themes/haze.css`, and its page background is dark. **Fix:** both early returns render `<AppWallpaper />` first; `preview/launchPreview.tsx` imports `haze.css` and draws the wallpaper too; test `draws the Haze backdrop behind onboarding…` in `App.agent.mounted.test.tsx`. **Avoid:** any screen App returns early needs the wallpaper in Haze, and a preview harness must import every look's CSS it claims to show.

## 2026-11-10 · Dropdowns opened behind the hire dialog

- **Symptom:** the Model list in the hire dialog opened behind the dialog. **Cause:** `Select` portals its list to `<body>` when it is inside a `[role="dialog"]` and gave it `zIndex: 200`, but the team dialogs (hire, staff a project) sit on `.team-dialog-backdrop` at z-index 400 (`teamPage.css`). **Fix:** `FLOATING_MENU_Z = 1000` in `Select.tsx` (above every dialog, below tooltips at 10000); `Select.dialog.mounted.test.tsx` asserts it is above 400. **Avoid:** a new overlay above 1000 must also raise `FLOATING_MENU_Z`.

## 2026-11-10 · The CEO said a hired employee was on no project

- **Symptom:** the sidebar listed Fifi under the Retinue project, but the CEO said Retinue had no one staffed and Fifi was casual. **Cause:** since Phase 9 hiring, a bot belongs to a project through its own `projectId` OR a `project_members` row (`project.hire`, or a first conversation there), and `projectBlocks` (`ProjectsSidebar.tsx`) counts both. The CEO's roster (`team.rs` `list_blobs`), its `freeOnly`/`project` filters (`manager_blobs`) and the workspace summary in its instructions (`knowledge.rs` `workspace_summary`) read only `projectId`. **Fix:** `team::hired_project_ids` + `team::projects_of` (projectId first, then memberships) used in all three; `manager_blobs` also returns `projectIds`; test `the_ceo_counts_a_hired_bot_as_on_its_project` in `main.rs`. **Avoid:** never decide project membership from `projectId` alone; go through `projects_of`.

## 2026-11-13 · A hook that listens for daemon events breaks every test that mocks the bridge without it

- **Symptom:** adding `useComputer` (which calls `listenForDaemonEvents`) to the rail failed 17 rail tests with "No listenForDaemonEvents export is defined on the mock". **Cause:** `vi.mock('../tauri')` replaces the whole module, and a test file only lists what its component used at the time. **Fix:** add `listenForDaemonEvents: async () => () => undefined` to that mock. **Rule:** when a shared component starts using another export of `../tauri`, grep the mounted tests that render it and add the export to each mock.

## 2026-11-13 · An outside page cannot be framed by the window

- The app's content security policy has no `frame-src`, so `default-src 'self'` blocks any frame to a server, and a server's sign-in cookie is not sent to a cross-site frame anyway. The computer's screen therefore comes through the daemon on loopback (`computer.view` hands back a `127.0.0.1` address with a ticket) and the window frames only that; `parseComputerViewAddress` drops anything that is not loopback. Watching is a transparent shield over the frame, so no input reaches it until the owner chooses Take control.

## 2026-11-13 · Gates · The App roster tests time out in a full parallel run on this PC

- **Symptom:** `npx vitest run` failed 44 to 89 tests, nearly all in `src/ui/App.agent.mounted.test.tsx`, with a different count each time. **Cause:** that file mounts the whole app 77 times; with the default worker count (or a cargo build running) each test passes the 5-second limit. It passes alone in about 30 seconds. **Rule:** run the full UI suite with `--maxWorkers=3` and never beside a cargo build; a varying failure count in that one file is load, not a regression.

## 2026-11-13 · Daemon · A proxied frame: axum route shapes and the headers that must go

The computer's screen (`computer.view`, `computer.rs`) is the first route on the daemon's listener outside `/v1/*`. Three facts cost a build each:

- **A trailing slash is a different path.** `/computer/view/{ticket}` does not answer `/computer/view/<ticket>/`, and `{*rest}` does not match an empty remainder. Register both the bare ticket and the ticket-with-slash to the same handler, then the catch-all below them.
- **Buffer and strip `Content-Length`.** The proxy reads the whole upstream body, so any copied `Content-Length` (or `Transfer-Encoding`) contradicts the body axum writes. Strip both, with `Set-Cookie`, `Content-Security-Policy*`, `X-Frame-Options`, and add `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors <app origins>`.
- **A frame cannot send the capability**, so the route is proved by a ticket in the path: 64 hex characters, compared in constant time against every live ticket, 10 minutes after last use, cleared on unlink. `desktop/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` must add `frame-src 'self' http://127.0.0.1:*` or the app's own CSP blocks the frame. A WebSocket to the same path is forwarded with `tokio-tungstenite` (workspace dependency, promoted from dev-dependencies with `rustls-tls-webpki-roots` for `wss://`).

## 2026-11-13 · Remote · Harbor's screen accepts the computer token

- The owner's Harbor (`/root/Dashboard/harbor/server/proxy.js` on the VPS, deployed to `/srv/harbor/app/server/proxy.js`) now lets a computer token open `/harbor/computer/view/` and its WebSocket, besides the owner's session, with one audit line per token every 5 minutes. The desktop is Selkies, and its client connects to `${base}/api/websockets?role=viewer`. A hand-made upgrade needs a correctly padded `Sec-WebSocket-Key`, or the container answers `400 Handshake error`. SSH to the VPS with `-o ClearAllForwardings=yes`: the `synthet-mail` host entry carries mail port forwards that fail when the ports are busy.
- **Shell trap (Director, same day):** never put Markdown with backticks inside a double-quoted `node -e "..."` in Bash. Bash runs each backticked span as a command; here it tried to execute `tiers.json` and a research note as scripts (harmless only because both failed to parse). Write such text with the Write or Edit tool.

## 2026-11-13 · Machines · Starting and steering work on a linked machine is a local pass-through

- The gateway already lets a `chat` device run `agent.conversation.create`, `session.prompt`, `session.steer`, `session.cancel` and `session.queue.*`, and a `viewer` device `project.list` (`apps/desktop/src/remote/tiers.json`); `permission.reply` is the `approve` class, so `operator`. What was missing was the local half, and it is a pass-through in `crates/retinue-daemon/src/machines.rs` (`machine.start`, `.prompt`, `.steer`, `.cancel`, `.permission.reply`, `.projects`), not a gateway change.
- **Copy the far side's class, not your own opinion of it.** One helper (`require_tier`) takes the far side's `minTier` per method and refuses with `tier_required` and one sentence; the gateway still has the last word, so its own refusal is returned unchanged. Check the class in `tiers.json` every time: `permission.reply` is `approve` at `operator`, everything else here is `chat` except `project.list` at `viewer`.
- **`movedTo` naming the machine you are asking is not a refusal.** `refuse_if_moved` refuses a conversation recorded as moved to another machine, and the giving machine keeps that `movedTo` for good after `commit`. Applying it unchanged to `machine.prompt`/`.steer`/`.cancel` would refuse the very conversation the owner just moved *to* that machine, so the local check clears `movedTo` when it names the machine being asked and keeps the live-lease refusal. A move elsewhere, or a lease, still refuses with the existing sentences.
- **A write must refresh the snapshot.** The 20 s loop would otherwise hide a just-started conversation, or a bot that just became busy, for up to that long. `write_through` calls `refresh_one` after the far side answers (reads such as `machine.projects` do not).
- **Pairing still needs `remote.pair.confirm`,** a window RPC, so a headless host (a Linux VPS) cannot approve a device yet. Research: `docs/research/many-machines-2026-11.md`.

## 2026-11-13 · Daemon · A `tracing` call written as a match arm breaks the log-site scan

- `fix39_p12_tests::every_log_call_site_is_a_fixed_message` slices each call site to the next `);` and refuses `token`, `message`, `content`, `command` and the like anywhere in that slice. An expression arm (`Ok(x) => tracing::info!("…"),`) has no `);` of its own, so the slice runs into the following code and fails on a word three lines away. Write log calls as statements ending in `);` (`=> { tracing::info!("…"); }`).

## 2026-11-13 · Computer · An employee's workplace

- `computerWorkplace` on an employee (`agents.computer_workplace`, added by `ensure_computer_link_schema` in `crates/retinue-storage/src/computer_link.rs`) adds a `## Your workplace` section to new conversations' instructions (`computer::workplace_instructions`, from `team::runtime_instructions_in`) and wakes a sleeping computer once per conversation at the top of `run_turn` (`computer::before_first_turn`, claimed through `Storage::session_take_computer_wake`, 20 s budget, never sleeps it). Only when a computer is linked and shared with employees. The window's switch is `ComputerWorkplaceRow` in `ui/BlobSettings.tsx`, shown only when a computer is shared or the switch is already on.

## 2026-11-21 ? Composer queue ? Resume appeared to do nothing

- Queue mutations in `ui/App.tsx` used a shared RPC wrapper with a global error and an empty catch. The composer showed no error or pending state. Keep queue feedback scoped to the conversation, clear it at the next queue mutation, and render it on the composer status line.
- `queue.rs::resume` clears the pause before attempting a drain and returns `paused: false` even if the drain re-pauses. Read `session.queue.list` after the mutation, and use visible turn, limit and item state to explain waits. A specific reason for an otherwise unexplained wait requires the daemon to return a drain outcome.
